# Changelog

*Layout.* Entries are newest-first within each section. The bullets directly
below record the `v0.1.1-qa.1` (2026-08-30) and `v0.1.1-qa.2` (2026-08-31) QA
prereleases cut from `main`; they sit above `[Unreleased]` because they describe a tagged QA
build, not a production release. `[Unreleased]` holds every batch merged since
`0.1.0-beta`, including the ones those QA builds packaged — no production
release has been cut, and the hosted world is not deployed. New batches go
under the first `[Unreleased]` heading (see `.claude/skills/changelog-entry`).

- **`v0.1.1-qa.2` is published, independently verified, and running for continued QA** (GitHub Releases, Release workflow run `33393926388`). The annotated tag points at merged `main` commit `32af091`; the tag-triggered workflow completed its full gate in 3m03s (**908 Python passed**, **95 Vitest passed**, production bundle byte-fresh, **14 Playwright passed** under the production CSP), built `enfolded-0.1.1rc2-py3-none-any.whl` once, installed and smoke-tested that exact file, and published it with SHA-256 `b6adbe44daae927ef963cde1b64e58f4ca05938d0b950c13a27411ab675d0fd0`. The attached assets were downloaded independently, the wheel matched `SHA256SUMS`, and a fresh isolated environment installed it as `enfolded 0.1.1rc2`. That installed release is running at `http://127.0.0.1:8080/app` against the prior isolated QA home so its 9,088-event test history remains continuous; browser smoke confirmed the rendered scene, connected session, sound-on default, directly opened durable solved-puzzle state, full chronicle, and zero console errors. The `v0.1.1-qa.1` release is titled superseded and links forward; its original four assets, sizes, and digests remain unchanged. **Irreversibility check:** the QA.2 tag/release is the deliberate durable distribution record already created; this follow-up changes documentation only — no runtime code, migration, golden re-pin, chronicle row/write path, world metadata, or packaged bytes.

- **QA.2 receives a unique package identity** (`pyproject.toml`). The package advances from `0.1.1rc1` to `0.1.1rc2`, satisfying the release workflow's one-tag/one-version contract for `v0.1.1-qa.2`. This candidate packages the merged QA corrections for durable puzzle rewards and state refresh, lower-friction navigation and discovery, and non-repetitive agent dialogue. Verified: Ruff green; **908 Python passed**; **95 Vitest passed**; production bundle byte-fresh; the built `enfolded-0.1.1rc2` wheel passed installed-artifact smoke; **14 Playwright E2E passed** under production CSP. No runtime code, golden world, migration, chronicle kind, or `world_mutations` write path changes in this version-only step.

- **Agent dialogue rotates before it repeats** (`agents/banter.py`). Co-located wanderers now cycle deterministically through opener, response, closing, and semicolon-separated node observations instead of independently re-rolling each meeting; each named cast member's signature phrase follows a deterministic one-in-three cadence and can never appear in adjacent meetings with the same pair at the same node. This preserves zero-cost generation and exact replay while removing the repeated observation-plus-tic pattern found in QA. Regression coverage pins adjacent spoken-line, aspect-fragment, and signature-cooldown variety. Verified: Ruff green; **908 Python passed**; **95 Vitest passed**; production bundle byte-fresh; installed-wheel smoke green; **14 Playwright E2E passed** under production CSP. No golden re-pin, migration, chronicle kind, or new `world_mutations` write path.

- **QA navigation and discovery use the click budget on play, not UI ceremony** (`/app`). Horizon nodes now load their inner passages automatically (with retry only on failure); the sidebar passage list is a keyboard-accessible navigation fallback for the canvas; the full chronicle and Player's Guide are promoted to clearly named controls; “Wayback” is now player-facing “Replay History”; and property values preserve whole words with language-aware hyphenation. Ambient sound is the default preference, activates on the first browser-permitted gesture, and remembers an explicit mute. Browser behavior tests cover automatic depth loading, passage navigation, promoted controls, renamed history replay, and persisted sound preference. No golden re-pin, migration, or new `world_mutations` write path.

- **QA core state is visible where players act** (`/app`, `/puzzle`). Opening the Puzzle tab now loads its puzzle immediately; revisiting a human-solved puzzle shows its durable solver, contributors, and pooled attempt state instead of resetting; a fresh solve names its material reward; and successful puzzle/verb mutations quietly refresh the canonical `/world` node so its Properties panel changes with the chronicle. Other players' live solve and immediate/matured action broadcasts trigger the same refresh. +1 HTTP integration test and +1 browser behavior test. No golden re-pin, migration, or new `world_mutations` write path.

All notable changes to this project are documented here.

---

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **Each text task can name its own provider, and the daily voice and image allowances use neutral names without replenishing a day already spent** (`consciousness/runtime.py`, `consciousness/model_check.py`, `main.py`, `server/guard.py`, `server/__init__.py`, `consciousness/__init__.py`, `consciousness/interventions.py`, `server/handlers.py`, `server/intervention_api.py`, `tests/test_task_providers.py`, `tests/test_budget_names.py`, `docs/development/agent-runtime.md`, `docs/roadmap/beta-launch-checklist.md`). `NESTED_WORLDS_PROVIDER` stays the process default and still means `anthropic` when unset, so an existing deployment that sets nothing new does not change and does not enable another destination. `NESTED_WORLDS_NODE_PROVIDER`, `NESTED_WORLDS_INHABITANT_PROVIDER`, `NESTED_WORLDS_INTENT_PROVIDER`, and `NESTED_WORLDS_MODERATION_PROVIDER` override that choice for one task. A blank, unknown, or unsafe name fails `python main.py models` and `python main.py serve` with one line before the report and does not fall through. An unsafe value is not echoed. A missing SDK names the variable that selected that adapter. Default model ids come only from that task's adapter: `openai_compatible` still has no `default_model`, and a task routed to it does not inherit `claude-opus-4-8` or `claude-haiku-4-5`. `NESTED_WORLDS_MODEL` remains a shared model id for node voice, inhabitant voice, and intention, including when those tasks use different providers. It is not a provider selector. Moderation never inherits it. An id the task's provider rejects is a hard startup problem. Using that shared id on a provider other than the process default is a soft warning. A provider with no credential still boots; `models` prints `provider not configured`, and names the tasks when the providers differ. `--probe` calls each configured provider's own ids. One unconfigured provider does not skip a configured provider's probe, and the skipped line still exits 1. In-flight slots stay per provider. A provider that is full or raising does not block, and is not retried on, a task routed elsewhere. New voice calls write `anthropic` and new image calls write `fal_ai`, including the existing `:u:<hash>` per-user keys. The public names do not rename those rows. `schema_version` stays 29. A schema-29 binary therefore sees the same spent allowance. A `voice` or `image` row left by the declined key-rename build still counts on this binary and is not copied or deleted; a schema-29 binary does not read it. Node voice, inhabitant voice, and intention share that one voice allowance whichever providers serve them. Moderation keeps `moderation`. Neutral caps are `NESTED_WORLDS_VOICE_DAILY_CALLS`, `NESTED_WORLDS_VOICE_DAILY_CALLS_PER_USER`, `NESTED_WORLDS_IMAGE_DAILY_CALLS`, and `NESTED_WORLDS_IMAGE_DAILY_CALLS_PER_USER`. The legacy `NESTED_WORLDS_ANTHROPIC_*` and `NESTED_WORLDS_FAL_*` names are still accepted. A blank value is unset. One integer wins. Both equal uses that integer. Both different uses the lower integer, logs a startup warning, and does not exit, so raising a cap while a lower legacy secret is still set cannot crash-loop the server. A non-integer or a negative value refuses the allowance (cap 0, no increment) and still exits without echoing the value. `0` is a valid cap and refuses every call. The new names do not match the secret-redaction pattern `(KEY|TOKEN|SECRET|PASSWORD|CREDENTIAL)`. Per-provider concurrency and slot-wait settings are not renamed. Checklist E3 moves from Not started to Done and stays Can follow. M5 stays Partial: routing is per task and there is still no failover. The held-decisions row records the public names as decided (owner-authorized Track 3, 2026-11-06) and records that the owner declined the storage-key migration the same day. A recount of all 95 item rows, after merging #152, is Done **13**, Partial **37**, Not started **42**, Unknown **3**. 54 block beta, and 44 of them are still open. Economy is Done 2, Partial 1, Not started 2. ADR-035 stays Proposed. No destination is approved. No live provider call.
  **Review corrections (Copilot, Codex, and Claude Code on #153 at `4e05684`):** `evals.harness.task_routing` routes `get_provider` and `provider_for` through the candidate for the whole context, including a task selection that was already cached, and ignores `NESTED_WORLDS_{NODE,INHABITANT,INTENT,MODERATION}_PROVIDER` until the context ends. A fixture run and a loopback live run with all four variables set and the selection pre-cached recorded the candidate's calls and left the operator adapter uncalled; the loopback run still reserved spend and kept the destination allowlist. Two valid voice or image caps that disagree log a warning and enforce the lower integer. `models`, `serve`, and `voice-health` print it and do not exit, so a leftover `NESTED_WORLDS_ANTHROPIC_DAILY_CALLS=500` beside `NESTED_WORLDS_VOICE_DAILY_CALLS=1000` does not crash-loop a restart. A non-integer or a negative cap still fails startup and is not echoed. Raising a cap sets the neutral name and the legacy name to the same integer in one `fly secrets set`. `fly secrets unset` is a separate command; `--stage` on each, then `fly secrets deploy`, is the documented way to apply both in one restart. The owner declined migration 0030 on 2026-11-06: restoring a pre-migration backup to keep schema 29 would discard world history, which is disproportionate to an internal key name. The file is removed. Charges stay on `anthropic` and `fal_ai`. The schema-29 writer in the test is the control flow from `18e5bbd`, with its own cap parser and 16-hex hash, and it does not call the current helpers. On one disposable database, spend before and after the switch stays spent. A neutral-only cap, a higher legacy alias, a cap of 0, and a malformed neutral value are included: the older writer ignores the neutral names until each effective cap is copied onto the legacy variable. A cold `provider_for` publishes the task source and name under one lock. A loser returns the winner's source, so a process-default winner is `get_provider` and is not loaded as `None`. The wheel gate compares migration names and bytes with the source tree and refuses a `build/lib` copy of a deleted or edited file. `main` at `f3ead74` (#152) is merged. E3 stays Done. A task that follows the process provider does not store a task-provider name. `--probe` calls `_probe_models` once per provider. `_consume_allowance` is the shared voice and image consumer. - **A same-schema release can be rolled back, and a binary refuses a newer chronicle before it listens** (`scripts/deploy.sh`, `persistence/schema_probe.py`, `persistence/__init__.py`, `server/__init__.py`, `main.py`, `tests/test_schema_guard.py`, `tests/test_deploy_rollback.py`, `tests/test_beta_checklist_counts.py`, `docs/infrastructure/incident-response.md`, `docs/infrastructure/fly-deployment.md`, `docs/infrastructure/delivery-recovery.md`, `docs/roadmap/phase-2-scale.md`). `known_schema_version()` is the highest well-named migration this binary ships (0001–0029, pin **29**). `init_db` raises `SchemaTooNewError` when any applied version is higher, before a migration runs and before `_initialized` is set. `server.run` does that same read-only compare first, then checks the canonical seed and the model configuration, and only then calls `init_db`. A bad seed or model id exits before any database file is created and before a pending migration is applied. The newer-schema path prints `error:` and exits 1 before the port, Sentry, or the heartbeat. The message names both versions and says there is no in-place downgrade. `python main.py schema-version` prints `known N` and `applied N` or `applied none` without creating or migrating the database, and exits 1 with one line and no traceback on a newer chronicle or an unreadable file. Boot and the deploy probe share `persistence/schema_probe.py`. `scripts/deploy.sh --rollback <image>` takes `python main.py backup --to /data/backups/worlds-<stamp>.db` first and stops if that backup fails. It then reads the live `schema_version` with `fly ssh console -C`, and the candidate image's migrations with `fly console --image` (ephemeral machine, `/bin/sleep inf`, no volume, no published port, auto-destroy). Both probes are prefixed with `env` because `-C` is exec'd, not a shell, and the whole `python3 -c` argument is double-quoted because Hallpass shell-splits that string before the exec. `init_db` repeats the newer-schema refusal from a read-only open inside the init lock, before `_connect` can set `journal_mode=WAL`. The guard inside `_run_migrations` still refuses after the writable open. `fly machine run` is not used: it does not stream guest stdout. The script runs `fly deploy -a "$APP" --image "$IMAGE"` only when the two numbers are equal. An older image is pointed at the disaster restore. A newer image is refused because it would migrate on boot. A normal deploy refuses a checkout older than the live chronicle and still allows a forward migration. `--first-deploy` skips the backup and the schema check and cannot be combined with `--rollback`. An empty `FLY_APP` or `FLY_BIN`, including a variable set to the empty string, exits 2 before `fly` is invoked. A `--rollback` image must be `registry.fly.io/<this app>:<tag>` or `registry.fly.io/<this app>@sha256:<64 hex>`. Another registry, another app, an empty tag, a flag-shaped ref, whitespace, `..`, or shell metacharacters never reaches `fly`. `FLY_BIN` selects the flyctl binary so the script is tested offline. Pruning to the newest five machine-local backups happens only after the schema check and does not block the deploy if it fails. The procedure, including the crash-loop case where SSH is already dead, is in the runbook and in delivery-recovery. The live staging redeploy under 10 minutes has not been run; it waits on a Fly app (checklist F1). F4 stays Partial. The one-page playbook `docs/infrastructure/incident-response.md` names the kill switches and caps in `server/guard.py`, the single-machine restart from `fly secrets set`, invite revocation and sanctions, moderation hold, `redact`, and when to roll back versus restore. Mark Weeks decides. Juries G3, G4, G6, G7 and G8 stay open and are not active. No held policy (minimum age, chat permanence, external destinations, world status, backup retention) is decided. F5 and P6 move from Partial to Done. The checklist recount is **95** rows: Done **12**, Partial **37**, Not started **43**, Unknown **3**. 54 block beta, and **44** of them are still open. P6's go/no-go box is checked. F4's is not. **13** tests in `tests/test_schema_guard.py`, **43** in `tests/test_deploy_rollback.py` (including each rejected image ref), and **1** checklist-count test. No Fly app was contacted.

- **The eval harness runs any registered text adapter, including `openai_compatible`, without a new default provider, model, or endpoint** (`evals/harness.py`, `scripts/model_eval.py`, `consciousness/runtime.py`, `tests/test_eval_provider_harness.py`, `evals/README.md`, `docs/development/agent-runtime.md`, `docs/roadmap/beta-launch-checklist.md`). A candidate's `provider` is the selection. `fixture` stays the offline path. Any other name must already be in `runtime.provider_names()`; an unknown name, including one that is not a short token, still fails with `provider has no evaluated adapter; no implicit fallback` and the message does not echo the value. The harness does not read `NESTED_WORLDS_PROVIDER`. `runtime.build_provider` instantiates the named adapter and does not cache it as the process selection. `anthropic` keeps a fresh client, `max_retries=0`, the candidate timeout, and the existing refusal of any base URL other than `https://api.anthropic.com`. Every other adapter, including `openai_compatible`, is the registry instance and is refused with `provider is unconfigured` when `configured()` is false. No base URL, model id, or credential is filled in. A live run also requires `--live` and both `--max-usd` and `--max-calls`; omitting either raises `live run requires an explicit spend ceiling` before a client is built and before an output directory is created (CLI exit 2). Each call record stores `provider`, the requested `model`, `resolved_model`, and `usage` as the adapter reported it, including `null` counts. Cost is a number only when the candidate's price map covers the model and every count is a non-negative integer with a rate. A missing count is not treated as zero. The known counts still produce a lower bound, and that floor is compared with the reserve before the cost is left unknown. If the floor exceeds the reserve, the floor is counted and further dispatch stops with `pricing_bound_exceeded`; otherwise the reserve stays. A loopback server that reported `prompt_tokens` 100, `total_tokens` 50100, `cached_tokens` 0, and no `cache_write_tokens` stopped after 1 request of 5 planned. At the test rates that floor is $0.1001. Five such calls would be about $0.5005, while five reserves of the 256-token voice bound are $0.00881 and fit under `--max-usd 0.01`. A non-anthropic live run refuses unless `--allow-destination HOST` equals the host of the resolved base URL. There is no default allowlist, no environment default, and no wildcard. The approved host is stored on the run and its provenance. On the loopback run that host is `127.0.0.1`; the URL with its port, and the API key, are not stored. A missing flag, a blank flag, `localhost`, `*`, a URL, and `127.0.0.1:9` each refused before any request and before an output directory. Setting an environment variable to the matching host did not approve the run. `anthropic` records `api.anthropic.com` when that client is configured. A passed `--allow-destination` is accepted only when it is exactly that host; any other value is refused. Destination approval for prompts that can carry player text is Mark Weeks' decision. The flag is the operator's approval for that one run. A live call with no explicit timeout uses the candidate `timeout_seconds`, including `openai_compatible`. Moderation's explicit 3 second timeout is kept. Against a loopback server that waits 3s, `timeout_seconds` 1 with the adapter timeout set to 30 finished as `ProviderTimeout` with trial latency under 2.5s, and the recorded request timeout was 1.  A loopback 500 kept `accounted_usd` equal to the reserve. A 500 body containing the API key left `error` as `ProviderHTTPError`, and the key was absent from the result and every output file. `build_provider` on an unknown name, including one that is not a short token, raises the same message and does not echo the value. `validate_candidate` accepts ` Fixture ` and ` Anthropic ` after normalizing. A price-map miss returns no cost and does not move the reserve. A missing `cache_write_per_million` is refused. The refusal does not say that explicit null is allowed. Explicit null and explicit zero are both refused for `anthropic`, because that adapter writes cache and either rate lets `--max-usd` undercount. At the test rates, 10 reported writes at an explicit zero settle at `16/1_000_000` (the input and output only); those writes at $1.25 per million would add `12.5/1_000_000`. Validation now refuses `0` and `0.0` for `anthropic` before a run. For any other provider an explicit null leaves cache writes out of the pre-call reserve. A positive write count then halts the run with the cost left unknown: 1000 writes at a null rate reserved $0.001512 (input rate only, 256-token voice bound) and stopped after one call instead of continuing. When the server reports no write count, the same null rate does not halt: 5 of 5 calls finished with cost null, and the result and its provenance record `cache_writes_bounded: false`. An explicit zero prices an unreported write count at zero and records `cache_writes_bounded: true`, so OpenAI-shaped usage (`cache_write_tokens` absent, `prompt_tokens` 100, `total_tokens` 110, `cached_tokens` 0) settled at `120/1_000_000` instead of staying null. `--allow-destination` matches the lowercased hostname only. A different case, a trailing dot, a substring, and a suffix do not match. It covers every port on that host. `anthropic` refuses any passed flag other than `api.anthropic.com`, including `API.ANTHROPIC.COM` and `api.anthropic.com.evil.com`. The run provenance and each call record store the candidate timeout and, for `openai_compatible`, the resolved reasoning effort, output-limit parameter, schema flag, and store flag. A moderation call records its own 3 second timeout on that call while the run still records the candidate's 10 seconds. The default store flag is recorded false, and setting it records true. The URL and the credential are not stored. A floor equal to the reserve does not halt. Fixture runs still record cost `0.0` as authored evidence. `RecordingProvider` forwards `supports_json_schema`, so a schema-off intention is `UnsupportedCapability` before the model-action cap, the voice budget, and the eval reserve: a loopback server received 0 requests, both charge spies stayed empty, and `dispatched_calls` stayed 0. On a loopback Chat Completions server, one node-voice call recorded `openai_compatible`, requested model `fixture-v1`, echoed model `echo-loopback`, usage `50/10/40/10`, and cost `86.5/1_000_000` at the test rates (50×$1 + 10×$2 + 40×$0.1 + 10×$1.25 per million). The same path with `prompt_tokens` 100 and `total_tokens` 110 and no cache split recorded input and both cache counts as `null`, output `10`, and cost `null`, with the reserve kept. The HTTP handler saw the eval reserve and the model-action charge already taken, and saw no voice-budget charge, because the harness's direct task calls still pass `voice_budget=False`. `OPENAI_BASE_URL` and `OPENAI_API_KEY` were set in the unconfigured test and were not used. Checklist M3 moves from Not started to Partial and stays Partial. Its evidence cell now names `--allow-destination`. At-a-glance counts move from Partial 38 / Not started 44 to Partial 39 / Not started 43 and do not move again. Open blocking items stay 45 of 54, because M3 is Can follow. ADR-035 stays Proposed. **42** tests in `tests/test_eval_provider_harness.py`. No live provider call. `origin/main` at `01398a0` (#145) is merged into this branch. - **The owner's November decision packet, a newcomer-watch facilitator kit, and the audit behind this week's batch are recorded, and the beta checklist is corrected to `23b8830`** (`docs/roadmap/decision-packet-2026-11.md` (new), `docs/evaluation/2026-11-02-newcomer-watch-kit.md` (new), `docs/evaluation/2026-11-02-week-plan.md` (new), `docs/roadmap/beta-launch-checklist.md`, `docs/roadmap/README.md`, `docs/roadmap/next-steps-2026-q4.md`, `README.md`). The audit records its method (five evidence readers; three planning lenses — critical path, irreversibility, player learning; two skeptics; then every cited line re-read at `23b8830`), the batch baseline measured before any PR in the batch (Ruff clean; **2,077 Python passed, 1 failed**, the `[timeout]` case of `tests/test_shared_text_fail_closed.py`, a WebSocket welcome timeout under parallel load whose file passed 68/68 three times alone; **138 Vitest**; bundle byte-fresh; installed-wheel smoke passed; `npm audit --omit=dev` one high, `@xmldom/xmldom` 0.8.13 with 10 advisories, fix 0.8.15), **15** confirmed findings with path:line evidence, the chosen week (the integrity PR, server-owned agent identities, this packet, reversible suspension and ban) and what waits and why. Two behaviours were re-executed on scratch databases outside the repository. After a shared puzzle's 3-attempt pool failed and released its answer, one more wrong guess wrote a second PUZZLE_FAILED (attempt 4) and the released answer then solved with `stabilized` (rows: 5 PUZZLE_ATTEMPT, 2 PUZZLE_FAILED, 1 PUZZLE_SOLVED). - **The owner can suspend a player for a set time or ban them, with a recorded reason, and lift either** (`persistence/migrations/0029_sanctions.sql`, `persistence/sanctions.py`, `persistence/__init__.py`, `persistence/participants.py`, `main.py`, `tests/test_sanctions.py`, `tests/test_model_provenance.py`, `tests/test_idea_promotion_review.py`, `docs/decisions/ADR-004-day-one-data-policy.md`, `docs/infrastructure/fly-deployment.md`, `docs/roadmap/beta-launch-checklist.md`, `docs/roadmap/README.md`, `docs/architecture/overview.md`, `README.md`). Checklist G5 (Blocks beta). Stacked on the invite-gate latch and per-frame socket re-check batch below. Until now `python main.py invite revoke` was the only sanction, and it was permanent: minting refuses any name held by any row, revoked ones included (migration 0011), `participants.rotate()` needs a live key, there is no un-revoke, and a re-mint creates a new participant whose journal and profile start empty. New owner-only commands: `sanction suspend <name> --hours N` (or `--days N`) `--reason "…"`, `sanction ban <name> --reason "…"`, `sanction lift <id> --reason "…"`, and `sanction list [--all]`. `<name>` is the registered name, ASCII letters in any case and other letters exactly as registered (the unique index's comparison), or the participant id. There is no HTTP surface. Migration 0029 adds two tables keyed to `participants.id`: `sanctions` (kind `suspension` or `ban`, reason, `created_at`, `expires_at`) and `sanction_lifts` (at most one per sanction, with its own reason and `lifted_at`). CHECK constraints refuse an empty or whitespace-only reason, an unknown kind, a suspension with no expiry or one ending at or before its start, a ban with an expiry, and any timestamp not in `datetime('now')`'s canonical form, so comparing the text compares the time (12 malformed-row cases in the tests). The application checks first: a reason is one line of 1 to 500 characters after trimming, with at least one visible character (owner review, below), and the library accepts a suspension of 1 minute to 365 days (a longer exclusion is a ban). The CLI takes whole `--hours` or `--days`, so its shortest suspension is 1 hour. The sign-off below keeps the cap. `persistence.sanctions` has no update or delete. A lift is a new row, and a sanction row never changes. Enforcement is one SQL clause, `persistence._UNSANCTIONED`, ANDed beside `revoked_at IS NULL` in `lookup_invite_key` (the HTTP gate, `registered_name` and the per-frame socket re-check) and in `participants._LIVE_INVITE` (`identify`: journal, profile, Ideas, act receipts, situations, interventions), and since the sign-off below also in `participants._PROFILE_NAME` (`profile`). A sanctioned key gets a response byte-identical to a revoked key's, `403 {"error": "forbidden"}`. Ideas, which authenticates on its own, gives its usual 403 "Open your current personal game invite to participate in Ideas." (asserted word for word). No reason, kind or end time reaches the player; notice and appeal are G8, a held owner decision. An open WebSocket closes with 1008 through the integrity PR's re-check, with the same bound as a revoked key: at once on a move or chat its rate limit admits, otherwise at its next complete frame once the 2 s re-check interval has passed, with each frame bounded by a 10 s deadline and a silent socket ending at the 60 s idle timeout (about 72 s worst case; tests run the interval at 0; tests for a ping and a chat, the second driven by the CLI). The sanction attaches to the participant, so rotating the key does not escape it. After a lift, or at the expiry itself with no operator action (a 3 s suspension, polled), the same key resolves to the same participant with notes and profile intact. A player who has never made an authenticated read has no participant row. Sanctioning by name creates it with `participants._ensure` semantics, so their first read finds that same participant (one row, tested). `invite_gate_active()` still reads only `credentials_ever_issued()`. With every live player sanctioned, keyless HTTP and WebSocket requests still get 403. Overlapping sanctions each have to end: lifting a ban leaves an active suspension in force. Sanctions write nothing to `world_mutations`. A closed socket writes the ordinary single `PLAYER_LEAVE` row, which does not mark a sanction. Logs carry the sanction id and kind only. A test captures every log record through a suspend, socket close, refused request, ban, lift and listing, and finds no reason text and no key. Cost of the clause, measured with a scratch script over 200 keys with 51 sanction histories (3000 lookups per run): **0.854** and **0.773** ms per `lookup_invite_key` with the clause, **0.695** and **0.857** ms for the bare query, on two runs. The difference is within noise. `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` shows an index search at every step (new index `idx_sanctions_participant`). Removing the clause from both reads fails **17** of the **65** tests on `bb5de93`, as the PR reviewer re-measured: the two Unicode target tests below depend on the clause too. This entry first said 15 of 63, counted before those two existed. With the owner review's tests it fails **21** of **100**, and **21** of **101** after that change's review. PR review (#138) found that the target lookup compared SQLite's ASCII-only `lower(trim(name))` with Python's Unicode `str.lower()`: `sanction ban Élodie` missed Élodie and, when `élodie` was also registered (the unique index allows both), banned `élodie`. The lookup now compares `lower(trim(name)) = lower(trim(?))`, exactly as the unique index does, so at most one row can match; two tests pin both cases and fail against the previous lookup (**65** tests in the file). Two existing tests needed edits. `test_model_provenance.py` pinned the top migration at exactly 28; it now asserts `>= 28`, so its byte-identical upgrade check covers 0029 too. `test_idea_promotion_review.py`'s upgrade test filled a version-24/25 database through `participants.identify`, which now reads 0029's tables. Its pre-upgrade phase now uses the pre-0029 liveness query, the same way it already avoided newer read paths. Migration numbers are assigned at merge (`docs/roadmap/README.md`, "Migration numbering rule"). 0029 was the next free number on this base. If another migration merges first, renumber the file and update the roadmap-index line that now names it as the top migration and the migration counts in `README.md` and `docs/architecture/overview.md` (now 29; both said 27, one behind `main`'s 28). Docs: runbook §6 "Suspending or banning a player" covers suspend vs ban vs revoke (revoke stays the permanent, name-burning action), timing, what the player sees and what is recorded. §7 now points there from redaction. Sanction reasons are private operator data that enter every backup, and `redact` does not reach them. The player-facing privacy notice must name this record, now noted on checklist L3. G5 is **Done** since the sign-off below. `tests/test_sanctions.py` has **106** Python tests: 63 before review on #138, 2 from the Unicode fix, 35 from the owner review, 1 from that change's review and 5 from the sign-off change.
  **Review pass (adversarial reviewer, branch `wk/pr4-suspension-r`, two code commits and this docs commit on top of the implementer's).** Four findings, all fixed. (1) Three operator inputs crashed `sanction` with an `OverflowError` traceback instead of a refusal: `suspend --hours 100000000000` and `--days 10000000000` (past what `datetime.timedelta` holds) and `lift 99999999999999999999` (past SQLite's INTEGER range). The first two now get the 365-day range message and the third "No sanction has id …", nothing written; three new CLI cases fail on the implementer's code and pass now. (2) The upgrade test pinned `0029_sanctions.sql` as the last migration and the post-upgrade version at 29, the same kind of pin this batch had to relax in `test_model_provenance.py`. It now finds the migration by name and accepts later ones; with a scratch `0030` migration present the old test failed and the new one passed. (3) This entry and runbook §6 quoted the Ideas refusal as "A current personal invite is required."; the endpoint actually answers "Open your current personal game invite to participate in Ideas.". Both now quote it, and the test asserts it. (4) The `_UNSANCTIONED` comment said every live-credential read carries the clause. `participants.profile` does not, so another player can still open a sanctioned player's published profile at `/profile` (200 with bio), while a revoked player's reads "That profile is unavailable." (400). Measured with a scratch script. The comment and runbook §6 then said so, and whether a sanction should hide the profile was left to the owner. The sign-off below hides it. Revert checks, each run against `tests/test_sanctions.py` with production code temporarily reverted (on the 60-test file before the review's tests were added; the three clause checks were repeated on the final 63-test file with the same counts): all production files back to `wk/pr1-integrity-r` (collection error, every test fails); the clause removed from `lookup_invite_key` only (**13** fail); from `participants._LIVE_INVITE` only (**6** fail); from both (**15** fail); lifts ignored in `_SANCTION_ACTIVE` (**9** fail); expiry ignored (**3** fail); `invite_gate_active` made to count only live, unsanctioned keys (**2** fail, including the gate-independence test); a reason added to the record log line (**1** fails, the log test); the migration's CHECK constraints dropped (**11** fail); the overflow fixes reverted (**3** fail).
  **Owner review on #138 (2026-11-02, branch `wk/pr4-fix` on `bb5de93`).** The review, posted from the owner's account, found no correctness or security bug and asked for the following. Each ask was checked against the code first. (1) G5 read Done before the owner's recorded sign-off on migration 0029. It went back to Partial until the sign-off below. (2) "Never logged" overstated. A reason given as `--reason "…"` lands in the operator's local shell history, and while the command runs it is in the process arguments of `flyctl` on the operator's machine and of `python` on the app machine. The `persistence/sanctions.py` docstring, the header comment of migration 0029 (comment only; the DDL is unchanged) and runbook §6 now say that the application's logs never carry a reason, and where a command-line reason does go. `--reason -` on `suspend`, `ban` and `lift` reads the reason from standard input and drops one trailing newline (`\n` or `\r\n`). A prompt goes to stderr only when stdin is a terminal. Input that is not valid text is refused with a message, and nothing is written. Runbook §6 shows `--reason -` from an interactive `fly ssh console` shell, with `cd /app` first because the image sets `HOME=/data`, and adds the shell-history line: `HISTCONTROL=ignorespace` or `ignoreboth` and a leading space (zsh: `HIST_IGNORE_SPACE`), or delete the entry. Piping a reason into `fly ssh console -C` is not documented, because whether flyctl forwards stdin there was not checked. (3) A reason of only `\u200b` was accepted, and `\x1b[2J` was stored and then printed raw by `sanction list`. `_clean_reason` now refuses every Cc character, tab and newline included, with "A reason is one line of text: tabs, newlines and other control characters are refused." It also refuses a reason with no character outside Unicode categories Z*, Cc and Cf ("A reason is required and must contain at least one visible character."). Lift reasons follow the same rule. A Cf character beside visible text is kept; an emoji joiner is one. The CHECK constraints are unchanged, so a row written before this change, or by direct SQL, can still hold a control character. Every `sanction` output line that shows a name or a reason now prints each non-printable character as its `unicode_escape` form (`\x1b`, `\u200b`). Printable characters, backslash included, print as they are. (4) Name matching is described the way the unique index compares: ASCII letters in any case, other letters exactly as registered (runbook §6 and above). (5) The suspend help said 1 minute to 365 days, but the CLI takes whole `--hours` or `--days`. It now says "1 hour to 365 days, whole hours or days", and the `suspend()` docstring names 1 minute as the library's minimum. (6) The Verified line records the later `check.sh` gates as reported, and the revert count is corrected from 15 of 63 to the reviewer's 17 of 65 (above). (7) **35** new tests, each revert-checked. Against the production files of `bb5de93` (`main.py`, `persistence/sanctions.py`), **26** fail: the 9 reason refusals, the lift-reason rule, two CLI refusal messages, the 6 `--reason -` cases for suspend and ban, the lift case, the terminal prompt, 5 stdin refusals and the escaped-output test. One of the 26, a reason of separators only, was already refused as empty and fails only on the new message. The other **9** pin behaviour that already held, so each was checked by the mutation it names. Exactly 365 days is accepted (`<=` to `<` on the maximum: 1 fails). Exactly 1 minute is accepted (the same on the minimum: 1 fails). A suspension whose `expires_at` equals SQL `now` is already over (`>` to `>=` in `_SANCTION_ACTIVE`: 1 fails, in 3 runs of 3). `sanctions.lift(True, "x")` is refused while sanction 1 exists, so the mutation would lift it (bool check removed: 1 fails). Suspending by participant id prints the registered name (printing the argument instead: 1 fails; printing the participant id fails 3). Ban and suspend print the session-close note (the note limited to suspend: 1 fails). A participant holding a revoked key and a live key shows the live key's name (ORDER BY flipped in `_ROW`: 1 fails; in `_resolve_on`: 1 fails). The expiry test writes `expires_at = datetime('now')` just after a second begins, then confirms that SQL `now` still equals it after its reads. An attempt that crossed into the next second is repeated, up to 10 times. It passed 8 of 8 repeated runs on the fixed code. (9) The `sanctions.resolve()` docstring now says it can create and commit a participant row. Not done, as directed: minting's Unicode normalisation (item 8, pre-existing, a separate follow-up) and merge notes.

- **An OpenAI-compatible Chat Completions adapter is registered for evaluation** (`consciousness/openai_compat_provider.py`, `consciousness/runtime.py`, `tests/test_openai_compat_provider.py`, `tests/test_provider_registry.py`, `tests/test_moderation.py`, `tests/test_shared_text_fail_closed.py`, `tests/test_expressive_interventions.py`, `tests/test_model_provenance.py`, `tests/test_model_check.py`, `consciousness/model_check.py`, `main.py`, `docs/development/agent-runtime.md`, `docs/architecture/overview.md`, `README.md`, `.env.example`, `evals/README.md`). `NESTED_WORLDS_PROVIDER=openai_compatible` selects it. Unset still means `anthropic`. The adapter is stdlib `urllib` only: no SDK, no default model id, and it does not read `OPENAI_*` or `XAI_*`. The operator sets `NESTED_WORLDS_OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_BASE_URL` (`https`, or `http` on `127.0.0.1`, `localhost`, or `::1`; userinfo, query, and fragment rejected) and `NESTED_WORLDS_OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_API_KEY` (required off loopback). Neither value is logged or placed in an exception message. Node voice, inhabitant voice, and intention use their task variable or `NESTED_WORLDS_MODEL`. Moderation needs `NESTED_WORLDS_MODERATION_MODEL` and does not inherit the shared model. With neither a task id nor that fallback, `python main.py serve` and `python main.py models` refuse before a port opens, with the existing `UnusableModelId` path. Strict `json_schema` is sent only when `NESTED_WORLDS_OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_STRUCTURED_OUTPUTS=1`. Otherwise `supports_json_schema()` is false and `runtime.generate` raises `UnsupportedCapability` before `reserve_model_action` and before the voice budget, so a schema-off intention sends zero requests and charges neither counter. An adapter that omits the method still accepts a schema. The caller still serves the authored fallback. Token accounting: ordinary input is `prompt_tokens` minus `cached_tokens` minus `cache_write_tokens` when that split is present (absent `cached_tokens` leaves input unknown; absent `cache_write_tokens` subtracts 0 and stays None); output is `total_tokens - prompt_tokens` when both exist and the difference is non-negative; otherwise output is unknown. `completion_tokens` is not a fallback, because it can omit reasoning tokens. Only `finish_reason` `stop` with non-blank string content and a null refusal is `complete`. Empty or whitespace-only content is not complete. A missing `refusal` counts as null. More than one choice, or content that is present and not a string, is `MalformedProviderResponse` and held. The request never sends `n` or `stream`, and only `choices[0]` is read. `length` keeps non-blank partial text. Every other finish, including `content_filter`, `refusal`, `tool_calls`, `function_call`, and xAI `end_turn`, sets text to None, because `require_text` publishes any string without reading `complete` — the plan table nulled text only for `content_filter` and `refusal`, and voice would otherwise have published those replies. HTTP errors, timeouts, any 3xx (not followed; `ProviderHTTPError` carries the status code only, so `Authorization` is not copied onto a request to another host), bodies over 1 MiB, malformed bodies, and connection failures hold shared text. Adapter exceptions are raised `from None` with a fixed message: no upstream text, body, URL, or key. Logs record the exception class only. Connection failures use `ProviderConnectionError` so a drop is not logged as a malformed body. An illegal max-output parameter (`NESTED_WORLDS_OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_MAX_OUTPUT_PARAM`, `max_completion_tokens` or `max_tokens` only; the name does not contain `TOKEN`, so `model_check` does not redact the value as a secret) or reasoning effort makes `configured()` false, so the voice handler does not spend the `anthropic` bucket on a typo; the plan defined `configured()` as URL plus key only. Reasoning effort is the closed set `none`, `minimal`, `low`, `medium`, `high` and is not forwarded otherwise. The opener installs `ProxyHandler({})`, so `HTTP_PROXY`, `HTTPS_PROXY`, `ALL_PROXY`, and the lowercase forms are not read. The caller's timeout is a wall-clock budget for one POST. urllib's own timeout is a limit on one socket operation; this adapter re-arms the socket with the time remaining and does not pass the caller's number through as that. Each resolved address is tried with the time still left, so three addresses do not spend the budget three times. A blackholed earlier address can consume the whole budget; later addresses are then not tried. The EBADF came from `_DeadlineSocket`, not from urllib. `makefile` created a `SocketIO` without counting it, and `close` closed the real descriptor at once. A stock socket defers that close while a reader exists. `http.client.getresponse` also closes the connection when `will_close` is true (`Connection: close`, HTTP/1.0, or no Content-Length). Copying the body out of a private `do_open` covered only that function's close, so those three replies, with the body 0.2 s after the headers, were still `connection failed`, and a body with no length past 1 MiB was `connection failed` instead of malformed. The wrapper now counts readers and closes the descriptor only after the last reader is gone. Stock `do_open` is used again. The fork that copied `req._tunnel_host`, `handler._debuglevel`, and `_check_hostname` is gone. A truncated body, announced size different from the bytes read, is malformed. A 500 whose Content-Length is over 1 MiB is `HTTP 500`. A key must be ASCII and printable and must not contain whitespace, so a CR/LF, an internal space, or a character outside ASCII leaves the adapter unconfigured on every host, including loopback, and is absent from `repr`. An absent key is still allowed on loopback. `https_open` forwards `check_hostname` only when that attribute is set, because Python 3.12 removed it from `HTTPSConnection` and from the handler. The same trusted loopback HTTPS call completed on Python 3.12.3 and 3.13.18. `generate` does not keep the `Bearer` header list as its own local. A host that cannot be encoded as IDNA is rejected. Deeply nested JSON is malformed at depth 100_000, which still raises `RecursionError` on Python 3.11, 3.12, and 3.13. `api_key` and the base URL are omitted from the settings repr. When `SENTRY_DSN` is set, Sentry's stdlib integration still records the outbound URL in the httplib breadcrumb it builds from the HTTP client span. The API key is not in that breadcrumb. An error response closes its socket before the adapter exception is raised, and a peer that closes before the status line is `ProviderConnectionError`. The POST budget is wall-clock. DNS lookup and individual TLS handshake bytes are not covered. One trusted HTTPS loopback call completes and sends the key. `python main.py models` prints `provider not configured` when the selected adapter is not configured, and does not echo the base URL. The earlier race failed `test_proxy_environment_is_ignored` on CI run 41366753117 (1 failed, 2005 passed) while the other run of the same commit passed. It reproduced here under CPU load, 3 of 20, before the first socket fix. A loopback reply that waits between the header flush and the body is a completion, including `Connection: close`, HTTP/1.0, and no Content-Length. Redirect targets are another listening socket on `127.0.0.1`, not port 9. After the fix, `TestUrllibLoopback` passed **200** times, eight at a time, with two CPU burners (2026-11-02T00:37:11Z–2026-11-02T00:40:51Z, 0 failed). The `anthropic` budget bucket and `NESTED_WORLDS_ANTHROPIC_*` names are unchanged; a voice call on this adapter charges that bucket once, and an unconfigured speak charges nothing. A model-written row stores `openai_compatible` and the model id after `_model_mark` (`persistence/__init__.py`): surrounding whitespace is stripped, and a non-string, a blank, a control character, or a value longer than 200 characters is dropped rather than stored or truncated. `python main.py models --probe` treats a `length` finish as accepted and records an exception as its class name. `docs/infrastructure/fly-deployment.md` is unchanged: no destination is approved for production. ### Fixed

- **A returning player whose browser holds no invite key is told how to return instead of waiting on "Opening the shared world…" forever, and the map's failed speech keeps the authored line** (`static/clientlogic.js`, `frontend/src/arrival.js`, `frontend/src/App.jsx`, `frontend/src/components/Interact.jsx`, `static/explorer.js`, `frontend/src/__tests__/arrival.test.js`, `frontend/e2e/keyless-return.spec.js`, `static/app/`). After the first load the invite key lives only in this browser's `localStorage` (`nw_beta_key`), because `frontend/src/auth.js` removes `key=` and `name=` from the address. A bookmark, a private window, another browser or device, or cleared site data therefore sends no key, and once the gate has latched `/world` answers `403 {"error": "forbidden"}`. On `18e5bbd`, `/app` threw on that answer, `loadWorld` returned false and `currentNode` stayed null, so the loading shell never cleared. On a new device the player first read the introduction and chose a name. The map printed the bare `forbidden` in its status bar over an empty map. The gate gives unknown, revoked and sanctioned keys the same 403 (`persistence.lookup_invite_key` returns None for all three), so the new shared rule `worldUnopened(status, hasKey, serverLine)` uses only what the browser knows. With no key, a 403 gives the invite line. With a key, a 403 gives a neutral refused line that names no cause; the stored key is kept, and opening another invite link replaces it through `rememberInvite`. Two answers keep the server's own line, as the map showed before: a 429 (the authored pace line) and a 400 (the map's depth refusal, `depth must be between 2 and 11 (got 12)`). Every other answer gives the retry line, including a JSON 404 or 401 from a proxy in front of the server, a transport failure, a 5xx and a non-JSON page. `openWorld(read, url, hasKey)` never rejects. Both clients call it. In `/app`, `arrivalScreen` takes `hasKey`. A browser that holds no key stays on the loading shell until `/world` answers, so with the gate latched it never draws the introduction or the name entry before the gate line, and `nw_seen_intro` and `nw_player_name` stay unset for the invite link. On an ungated server such a browser sees the introduction one `/world` round trip later than before. A browser with a key still reads the introduction while its world loads. The retry line waits until onboarding is done and offers "Open the view again". The button now switches to the loading shell as soon as it is clicked, before the position hydrate, so a second click cannot start a second arrival. A `/world` answer that opens but cannot be drawn (a 200 with no tree, where `entryPath` throws) now gives the retry line too; before, it left the shell up with no read in flight. The loading shell now appears only while a read is in flight. If a later read fails while a place is shown, the scene stays; the chat-restore case that expects the shell during a deepening jump still passes. The map shows the world line in its status bar, and also in the place description while nothing is selected. While nothing is drawn, Speak, Observe and the puzzle read leave that line in the status bar instead of replacing it with "Select a node first." The map's `/speak` and `/agent/voice` transport failure used to print `'Network error: ' + e.message` in the place-voice box and `Error` in the status bar. Both now show `SPEAK_UNREACHED_LINE`, the line `/app` already used; `Interact.jsx` reads it from `static/clientlogic.js`. The map's existing retry line is now `WORLD_UNREACHED_LINE`, also in `/app`; the text is unchanged and `ux-review.spec.js` still pins it. Not changed: the map still opens its introduction and join modals before its first `/world` read, so a first-time keyless visitor there chooses a name (saved as `nw_player_name`) before the invite line appears, and the heading still reads "Select a node". Reading `/world` first on the map, as `/app` now does, is left for a follow-up. Server, gate and 403 body are unchanged. Bundle `index-BLyml6G6.js` 345,672 B → `index-Dr1o1-gq.js` 347,553 B (+1,881 B; 109.13 kB gzip), rebuilt under Node 20.19.0. New tests: **12** Vitest cases and **6** Playwright cases. The Playwright cases run against the real server, with a minted key latching the gate. The Vitest cases cover a keyless 403 giving the invite screen rather than the shell, a refused key, a network failure, onboarding first, 5xx and non-JSON pages, the pace line and the depth refusal, a proxy's 404, 401 and 418, success, the in-flight and scene-kept cases, a keyless browser held on the shell until the gate answers, and a browser with a key reading the introduction while it loads. The new-device `/app` case holds `/world`, asserts that "Begin" and "Your name" are absent while it is held, and checks a `MutationObserver` record that neither was drawn before the gate line. Another `/app` case answers `/world` with a 200 `{}` body, then holds the position hydrate and double-clicks the retry: the shell shows at once, and exactly 1 hydrate GET and 1 `/world` read follow. A review of the first commit (`10093d1`) found that the gate did not come first when `/world` was slow (on loopback the introduction drew at 52 ms and the gate at 87 ms, and with `/world` held a click on Begin stored `nw_seen_intro` and showed the name entry), that a 200 body with no tree left the shell up, that a proxy's 404 or 401 text would reach the player, that the retry gave no feedback and took repeated clicks, and that the map's Speak replaced the invite line. All five are fixed in the second commit. Each fix was removed alone on the final code, with the bundle rebuilt where it applied, and the matching case failed: without the keyless hold the new-device case failed at the shell assertion, and with that assertion also removed it failed with "Begin" present (count 1); without the immediate shell the retry case failed at the shell assertion; without the retry line in the catch it found no alert; and without `needPlace` the map's `#status` read "Select a node first.". With `App.jsx` and `arrival.js` from `10093d1`, both new `/app` cases failed, and 2 of the new Vitest cases fail with `arrival.js` and `clientlogic.js` from `10093d1`. With `static/app`, `static/explorer.js` and `static/clientlogic.js` from `18e5bbd`, all **6** Playwright cases failed (2026-11-06T15:37:03Z). The first commit's screens were captured at 1280×800 and 390×844 and inspected. For the second commit the `/app` keyless wait and gate line were captured at 390×844 and inspected. The map shows the line twice: the status bar is the live region, and the description is where a reader of an empty map looks.

  **Review corrections (Copilot review on #154, confirmed by the owner's Codex review, 2026-11-07):** two findings, both reproduced and fixed. (1) A 403 whose body is not the server's own JSON (a proxy's HTML refusal, or a cut-off body) was read as the invite gate, so a browser holding a valid invitation was told its invitation does not open the world and lost the retry button. `openWorld` now treats an unparseable body as a failed read and gives the retry line; only the server's JSON answer speaks for the gate. (2) On the map, a `200 {}` from `/world` set `worldParams.seed` to `undefined` before drawing failed, so the retry line was not kept for Speak and Observe, and a later 403's line was dropped by the `seed === null` check. The draw-failure path now keeps the retry line in the place description and for Speak/Observe whenever no place is selected, and the gate line is kept whenever no place is selected (`!selected`) rather than by the seed check. New regression tests: a Vitest case for an HTML and a truncated 403 (with and without a key); a Playwright case for `/app` given a proxy's 403 page (retry shown, then the world opens on retry); and a Playwright case for the map given `200 {}` and then a gate refusal.   **Irreversibility check:** none. There is no migration, no golden re-pin, no new `world_mutations` write path, no `world_meta` pin and no change to the era banks. The diff is client-only: shared rules in `static/clientlogic.js`, the `/app` arrival screens, the map's world-load, speech and select-first status lines, the rebuilt bundle and tests. The server's invite gate and its 403 stay plumbing. Every line this batch adds or moves is authored, and the gate line replaces the loading shell in place, so no new surface is added.

- **A chat line the screen holds or declines comes back to the player's empty chat input, in both clients** (`server/websocket.py`, `static/clientlogic.js`, `static/explorer.js`, `frontend/src/chatDraft.js` (new), `frontend/src/App.jsx`, `frontend/src/components/TextPanel.jsx`, `frontend/src/ws.js`, `static/app/` (rebuilt), `tests/test_moderation.py`, `frontend/src/__tests__/chatDraft.test.js` (new), `frontend/e2e/chat-restore.spec.js` (new)). Both clients cleared the input on send, so a held or declined line had to be retyped. #147 makes holds more common: a classify that meets one transient provider error now holds an escalated line at once. The client could not tell which line a `chat_declined` answered. The reply carried only the authored line, and a rate-limited chat (`chat_bucket`, 0.5/s, burst 5) gets no reply at all. A "last line sent" guess would therefore restore the wrong line when two were in flight. A FIFO of in-flight lines would desync for the rest of the socket after one silent drop. **Wire addition:** a chat frame may carry a `ref`. The sender-only `chat_declined` echoes it on both refusal paths (screen verdict and the non-string refusal) when it is a string of 1 to 64 characters; any other value is not echoed. The ref is never broadcast and never stored: the `PLAYER_CHAT` row is still `{"text"}`. **One rule, shared** (`EnfoldedClient.createChatDraft` and `restoreChatInput`): each send records `{ref, text}` as the most recent line. A `chat_declined` whose ref matches returns that text once, and it fills the input only if the input is exactly `""`. Anything typed since, including whitespace, is kept. A reply for an earlier line, or one with no ref (a page cached before this change), restores nothing, which is today's behaviour. **Held and declined are treated alike.** Both authored lines ask the player to "say it another way", which needs the words to edit. Ideas also keeps the draft after both a hold and a content decline (`static/ideas.js`, the settled path). The text goes back only to its own author, and a resend is screened again under the same chat bucket, so restoring is not a bypass. The authored lines are unchanged and no text was added. **React wiring:** TextPanel still owns the input. Lifting it into App would re-render the unmemoized SceneView on every keystroke. App holds the draft and a `createChatRestore()` mailbox (since the review pass below). TextPanel attaches a pure `setChatInput(current => restoreChatInput(current, line))` to it in an effect, which is safe under StrictMode. `sendMessage` now returns whether the frame reached an open socket. TextPanel clears only then; before, a send while the socket was closing cleared the line and dropped it. The explorer already checked for an open socket. It now writes the input only when the value changes. **Known gaps (not addressed):** a rate-limited chat is still cleared and vanishes, with no reply to correlate. A socket that closes between send and verdict loses the line. An earlier line held while a later one is in flight is not restored, by design ("most recent"). Tests: **+2** Python (`TestChatScreening`: a block and a classify-error hold each echo the ref only to the sender, with the other player's first chat frame being the clean line and the row `{"text": "clean words"}`; `None`, `7`, `True`, `""`, 65 characters, a list and an object are not echoed, while 64 characters and the non-string refusal are). **+9** Vitest tests drive the shared rule through the real `dispatchMessage`: restore after a hold and after a decline, then resend with a new ref; typing kept; whitespace kept; at most once; only the latest of two; stray or missing refs ignored; no send. **+4** Playwright tests run both `/` and `/app` against the real server, with a block-list word declined and a watch word held because AI is disabled. They cover restore after hold and decline, no restore after a carried line, and, with `chat_declined` held back by `routeWebSocket`, typing kept and only the later of two lines restored. Revert checks: the two Python tests fail on `1aa389f`'s `server/websocket.py`, and all four browser tests fail on `1aa389f`'s `static/explorer.js` and `static/app` bundle (`toHaveValue`). Screenshots of both clients after a hold show the hold line in the feed and the line back in the focused input.
  **Review pass (GitHub Copilot on #151, medium, confirmed).** A verdict that arrived during a deep traveler jump was lost. `jumpTo` toward a node below the fetched horizon calls `loadWorld` without `background`, which sets `loading`. App then renders the loading shell in place of the panel, so TextPanel unmounts and its refill callback is gone. The socket stays open, so `declined()` consumed the line and nothing wrote it. Fixed with `createChatRestore()` (`frontend/src/chatDraft.js`, `/app` only, because the explorer's input never unmounts). A returned line goes straight to the mounted input, or waits for the next one to mount, where the empty-input rule still applies; it is given back at most once. A detach from an older mount cannot unhook a newer one. TextPanel attaches it in an effect. StrictMode's attach, detach and attach of one input fill it once. **+4** Vitest cases cover this: delivered while mounted; during the loading shell; across StrictMode; null and stale detach. **+1** Playwright case runs `/app` against the real server. It holds `chat_declined` back and injects a traveler at an 8-digit address, then clicks them and holds `/world`. The hold is released while "Opening the shared world…" shows, and the test waits until the page has handled the frame before letting `/world` through. After the remount the hold line is in the feed and the line is back in the input. On the pre-fix sources (bundle rebuilt from them), that case fails at `toHaveValue`; with the fix it passes.

- **A busy or failed screen on chat, Ideas, a published profile, a registered name or puzzle text now shows in `python main.py voice-health` and fails it** (`server/observability.py`, `server/moderation.py`, `consciousness/__init__.py`, `persistence/__init__.py` (`in_transaction`), `main.py`, `scripts/beta_metrics.py`, `tests/test_moderation_outcomes.py` (new), `evals/harness.py` and `tests/test_model_evals.py` (review), `README.md`, `docs/infrastructure/fly-deployment.md` §8 and §10, `docs/roadmap/beta-launch-checklist.md` (held-decisions row, B3), and #144's entry below). Follow-up to finding 2 of #144's owner-account review. `server/moderation.py` logged one INFO `moderation_call outcome=... surface=...` line per screen that reached a refusal or the classifier, and nothing counted it. `voice-health` read only the voice counters, so a screen that could not judge shared text reached the operator only when it held a voice line, and runbook §8 told the operator to search the logs. Each logged line is now counted where it is logged: in every `_moderation_log` call (the disable switch, a local block, the three per-user caps, moderation's budget, a missing moderation model id, a classify that raised or found no provider slot) and on the finished verdict `classify_content` logs (allow, block, undecided). So a screen counts at most once. A classify that finishes is counted by `classify_content`, and `screen()` then returns without logging. An exception is counted once, by `screen()`. A locally clean line is still neither logged nor counted, so a clean chat line costs no write and `allow` counts classifier allows only. The counter, `observability.moderation_outcome(surface, outcome)`, bumps `cost_budget` rows under a new `moderation_outcome:<surface>:<outcome>` prefix with the voice counter's posture: it never raises, waits at most 50 ms on the store (`_VOICE_COUNTER_WAIT_MS`), logs a WARNING on `nested_worlds.moderation` when a count is lost, and holds no player text, key, name or address. One count measured a median **1.12 ms** (p95 1.64 ms, 300 writes to a scratch WAL database on this 4-core host while other test runs were active). Surfaces and outcomes are closed sets, so bucket names stay bounded: 11 surfaces (`speak`, `agent`, `chat`, `ideas`, `profile`, `puzzle`, `name`, `cli`, the defaults `shared` and `classify`, and `other`) and 8 outcomes. An unknown surface is counted as `other`; an unknown outcome is dropped with a WARNING that omits its value. Grep found two surfaces the brief did not list, both included: `cli` (`interface._speak_to` writing the canonical world) and `name` (`name_refusal`'s logged block and disabled lines). Failures are `error`, `busy` and `undecided`, the meaning of the voice reason `screen`; limits are `budget`, `user_cap` and `disabled`. `voice-health` prints a "Shared-text screens" section after the voice section (per day and surface, the screens counted and then each outcome but `allow`, then the day's moderation budget use and the moderation switch) and adds "N screen failure(s) in the window" to its exit-1 problems. A failed screen on `/speak` or `/agent/voice` appears in both sections and the two counts are reported separately, never merged. `scripts/beta_metrics.py` reports the counts as `moderation_outcomes` beside `voice_outcomes`. **Transactions:** no caller screens inside an open SQLite transaction. `/speak`, `/agent/voice`, the puzzle guess (before `puzzle_attempt`), WebSocket chat (before `record_mutation`), Ideas (`ideas.submit` runs its `review` before `db.transaction()`), the profile save, `/register`, the mint and play CLIs and `interface._speak_to` all screen outside one. `increment_cost_calls` opens its own connection, so a count made inside this thread's `persistence.transaction()` would wait the 50 ms on the caller's own write lock and then be lost. The counter checks the new `persistence.in_transaction()` and skips the write at once with a WARNING instead. The budget and cap charges `screen` makes before a classify would still wait their 5 s there, as before this change. **Readers checked**, so the new rows inflate no spend or call figure: `persistence.get_cost_calls` (exact bucket: the guard caps and `voice-health`'s budget lines), `persistence.cost_rows` (a literal `substr` prefix: the voice and moderation summaries), `guard._consume` and `_consume_per_user` (their own bucket's new value), `scripts/beta_metrics.py` (a `substr` prefix; its `SUM(calls)` is the only sum, and no reader sums all buckets), the tests that read `cost_budget` (`test_moderation.py`, `test_shared_text_fail_closed.py`: exact names or other prefixes), and runbook §8's raw `SELECT * FROM cost_budget ... LIMIT 20`, which sums nothing but now lists more counter rows; it gains a sentence saying those buckets are counts, not spend. **Owner confirmation:** #144's entry now records the owner's 2026-11-04 confirmation of its three delegated choices, and the checklist's held-decisions table gains a Decided row for them. No repo text presented them as awaiting the owner, so nothing else changed. The decisions themselves are unchanged. Reversible: the counting is two call sites and one function, and removing them leaves dated rows no reader counts. **39** new tests run the real screen, the real classify through `runtime.generate` and the real per-provider semaphore with #144's fixture provider. They cover each outcome path through `screen` counting exactly once for its surface (local block, disable switch, three caps, budget, missing model id, provider error, busy, classify allow, block and undecided; the classify verdicts and the provider error also leave exactly one `moderation_call` line), a local clean line and empty text counting nothing, names and puzzle excerpts; a busy screen on WebSocket chat, Ideas, a published profile and a stored puzzle guess, each through the endpoint with a player key, counting `busy` under its surface with no voice outcome, and the player's words and key absent from every log line and bucket name; a failing counter store keeping the verdict (local block, classify allow and block, busy) and the player's chat and Ideas responses; a held write lock costing the screen under 1 s; a screen inside an open transaction skipping the write while the outer write still commits; the closed sets; the new rows leaving every spend bucket, the voice summary and `beta_metrics`' voice counts at zero; `voice-health` exiting 1 on only a chat busy screen, on `error` or `undecided`, and on a voice-surface failure (reported in both sections), exiting 0 on limits, allows and a block, and its window; and `beta_metrics`. Blocking calls run on a daemon thread with a join limit. **Revert checks**, each applied to the committed code, run against the new file and reverted: no count in `_moderation_log` failed **21** of 39; no count in `classify_content` **6**; `busy` removed from the failures, and so from the known outcomes, **13**; `busy` moved to the limits **4**; `screen()` also counting the classify verdict (a double count) **6**; no open-transaction skip **1**; the counter write without the 50 ms wait **1**; `voice-health` ignoring screen failures **6**; an unknown surface kept verbatim **1**.

- **Voice calls on the anthropic adapter give up on a provider that sends nothing after about 3 minutes, not about 30; a reply that keeps trickling can take longer** (`consciousness/anthropic_provider.py`, `tests/test_anthropic_timeout.py` (new), `tests/test_model_runtime.py`, `server/observability.py` (comment), `README.md`, `.env.example`, `docs/development/agent-runtime.md`, `docs/infrastructure/fly-deployment.md` §8). Node voice (`/speak`, 256 output tokens), inhabitant voice (`/agent/voice`, 200) and Act intention (500, with a JSON schema) pass no timeout to `runtime.generate`. The anthropic adapter therefore sent none, and the pinned SDK (anthropic 0.120.2) computed its own: 600 s read, 5 s connect (`resources/messages/messages.py:1030`, `_base_client.py:769-782`), with its default two retries (`_constants.py:9-10`). A provider that accepted the request and never answered held each in-flight slot for about 30 minutes (3 × 600 s plus at most 1.5 s of backoff), and #144's `busy` was the only outcome recorded meanwhile. Against a hanging local stub, the adapter at `6fa4288` sent `x-stainless-timeout: 600` and was still on its first attempt after 5 s of observation. **The change (decided design for this follow-up, reversible):** a new adapter-owned setting, `NESTED_WORLDS_ANTHROPIC_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`, default **60**, parsed like `NESTED_WORLDS_OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (unset, blank, non-numeric, non-finite, zero or negative means 60), capped at `threading.TIMEOUT_MAX` (review pass below) and read on every call. When the caller passes no timeout, the adapter sends `httpx.Timeout` with read, write and pool set to the value and connect at `min(5, value)`, so the SDK's 5 s connect is kept; httpx is imported lazily, as the SDK is. SDK retries for these calls stay at the default 2, so a transient 529 on a node voice is still retried. The worst case per call is 3 × 60 s plus at most 1.5 s of backoff, about **181.5 s**, when every attempt hangs. It is about **300 s** when the first two attempts answer an error just inside the read timeout with a retry-after of 60 s (the SDK honours 0 < retry-after ≤ 60, `_base_client.py:828`) and the third hangs. The same cases took about 1,801.5 s and 1,920 s before. Read is the longest wait for the next bytes, so a reply that keeps trickling can run longer. The caller-timeout path (moderation's 3 s classify, and the 30 s of the opt-in `--probe` of `models` and `voice-health`, which server startup never runs) is unchanged; follow-up A owns it. Setting the variable to 600 restores the old per-attempt timeout without a code change. **One addition to the brief:** a client whose own timeout is not the SDK default keeps it, and the adapter sends nothing. This is the same check the SDK makes at `messages.py:1030` before computing its 600 s. The evaluation harness builds its client with `with_options(max_retries=0, timeout=timeout_seconds)` (`evals/harness.py:486`), so without this exception a live run would silently use 60 s while its recorded candidate says otherwise (the template says 30). Production builds `Anthropic()` with the default, so the setting applies there. Deleting two lines in `_default_request_timeout` reverses it (mutant M3 below). **Why 60 is a judgement, with an evidence gap:** no live latency for these tasks is on record. `docs/evaluation/` and `evals/` hold no live run (no `run.json` or `trials.jsonl`), `evals/reporting.py` computes p50/p95 only from such runs, and the 2026-10-05 model evaluation lists hosted latency as needing live or staging evidence. The default rests instead on three things: the largest output budget (the intention's 500 tokens); the sibling adapter's 60 s default; and the asymmetry between the two errors. A timeout that is too short turns a slow but working reply into a timed-out attempt that is retried and possibly billed more than once, which is worse than a longer hang window. Revisit with measured p95/p99 per task. **Measured** with the real SDK against a local stub on 127.0.0.1 through `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, setting 0.5 s (the script is in the session scratchpad and not committed). A hanging stub saw 3 attempts (retry-count 0, 1, 2, each `x-stainless-timeout: 0.5`) and failed with `APITimeoutError` after **2.71, 2.80 and 2.82 s** in three runs, against an expected 1.5 s plus 1.125 to 1.5 s of backoff (0.5 s and 1 s, each scaled by 0.75 to 1.0). A 529 with retry-after 0.25 on every attempt was retried at +0.258 and +0.511 s and raised `OverloadedError`. A normal reply took 1 attempt (0.042 s on the first run, 0.003 s after), and with the setting unset the request header was `x-stainless-timeout: 60.0`. The `busy` comment above `VOICE_FAILURES` and runbook §8 now state the new hung-provider window, and that such a call ends as a `provider` failure. `busy` stays a failure (owner, 2026-11-04). Runbook §10 cites no SDK timeout and is unchanged. The 2026-11-03 decision-pushback evaluation's "600 s timeout" sentence is a dated record and is left as written. **23** new tests in `tests/test_anthropic_timeout.py` (26 after the review pass below): (1) the setting, where 10 unusable values send 60 with connect 5, 4 usable values are sent with connect `min(5, value)`, it is read on every call, and an intention-shaped call (500 tokens, a schema) gets the default and keeps its `output_config`. (2) Through the real SDK on `httpx.MockTransport`, the request carries connect 5 and read, write and pool 60 with `x-stainless-timeout: 60.0`; a caller's 3 s reaches the request unchanged; and a client with its own timeout keeps it. (3) Against the local stub at setting 0.3: a hang fails with `APITimeoutError` after 3 attempts within [0.9, 3.9) s; a 529 with retry-after 0.2 followed by a reply succeeds on the second attempt; and a working stub is answered in one attempt. (4) `/speak` through the real server with a hanging stub answers HTTP 200 with the node's authored silence and `ai: false` within the bound. It counts `voice_outcome:speak:provider` once and no `busy` or `ok`, logs `voice_fallback surface=speak reason=provider exception=APITimeoutError`, and gives back both slots. Blocking calls run on a daemon thread with a join limit, reusing `_bounded`, `_free_slots` and `_count` from `tests/test_provider_slot_wait.py`. `test_anthropic_keeps_missing_text_and_usage_unknown` now expects the default timeout in the request. **Revert checks:** each mutant was applied to `a770a9b`, run against the new file and `tests/test_model_runtime.py` (71 tests), and reverted. No default sent (M1): **21** failed, and instead of hanging, the stub hang test failed at its 10 s join limit and the `/speak` test at its client's 10 s timeout (10.01 s, re-run alone). Connect equal to the value (M2): **15**. Client-timeout exception removed (M3): **1**. SDK retries disabled on this path (M4): **3**. Setting read once per process (M5): **8**. Setting overriding a caller's timeout (M6): **8**. Parsed value not validated (M7): **6**.
