- **The invite gate stays closed once any credential is issued, and a revoked credential loses its open socket** (`server/guard.py`, `server/websocket.py`, `server/rooms.py`, `server/protocol.py`, `server/handlers.py`, `persistence/__init__.py`, `tests/test_ws_credential_lifetime.py`, `tests/test_server_protocol.py`, `tests/test_guard.py`, `tests/test_server.py`, `tests/test_server_rooms.py`, `tests/conftest.py`, `README.md`, `docs/architecture/overview.md`, `docs/infrastructure/fly-deployment.md`, `docs/roadmap/beta-launch-checklist.md`). This fixes four confirmed findings. (a) `invite_gate_active()` returned `bool(list_invite_keys())`, which counts active rows only, and returned False on any exception. Revoking the last live key reopened the world to keyless play, a database read error opened the gate, and an operator who had only created `invite create` registration links, with no minted key, was running an open world. The gate now reads `persistence.credentials_ever_issued()`: two `EXISTS` probes over every `invite_keys` row and every `registration_tokens` row, whether revoked, redeemed or cancelled. Once true, it stays true. On an exception the gate counts as closed, and the error is logged at error level by exception class only. A database where nothing was ever issued stays open. (b) The WebSocket was authorized once, at upgrade. A socket that upgraded with a live key now re-reads that key (one `lookup_invite_key`) before any move or chat its bucket admits writes or broadcasts, and on every other client frame at most once per `guard.WS_REAUTH_INTERVAL_SEC` (2 s). Any other socket re-checks the gate the same way. The first version of this batch checked only a move or chat its bucket admitted and application pings. Review found that any other frame (a protocol ping or pong, an unknown type, invalid JSON, a non-object, an empty text frame, or a throttled move or chat) still reset the 60 s idle timer unchecked. A revoked client sending one of them every 50 s stayed open indefinitely and kept receiving every room broadcast; a probe showed it received another player's chat line sent after the revocation. A browser client now closes within one 20 s keepalive of revocation. A socket that sends nothing is not checked and keeps receiving room broadcasts until the 60 s idle timeout ends it; the second review round below bounds the rest. Each check opens one SQLite connection, measured at about 0.72 ms per `lookup_invite_key` and 0.59 ms per gate probe on this machine. Checking every frame, as the review version did, let any invited client turn a frame flood into one connection per frame; the 2 s throttle on frames that write nothing bounds that, and the fresh check before a move or chat keeps a revoked credential from writing inside the interval. In the throttle tests, 76 junk and protocol-ping frames cost **1** lookup (the per-frame version: 76), and a chat or move sent inside a 60 s interval right after revocation still closes with 1008 and writes nothing. Tests run with the interval at 0 (`tests/conftest.py`) so a suite can revoke and send one frame; `TestReauthorizationThrottle` restores real intervals. A failed check stops the writer, sends close status 1008 through the new `protocol.send_close`, and leaves through the existing disconnect path. Review also found that the writer thread could write a frame it had already dequeued after the close frame. `Player._drain` now re-checks the dead flag under the send lock that the close frame is written under. The refused frame writes no row and broadcasts nothing. The usual single `PLAYER_LEAVE` row and `player_leave` broadcast follow. A rotated key closes the same way, because rotation rewrites the row's digest. A failed key read also closes the socket. (c) Chat and move buckets were per connection, so the 8-sockets-per-IP cap let one credential post 8 times the chat burst. Keyed sockets now share `CredentialBuckets`: module-level and lock-protected, keyed by `sha256(key)[:16]`, and bounded at 4096 identities. Fully refilled buckets are evicted first, then the least recently used. Keyless dev sockets keep their own pair. Measured with a scratch script, 8 sockets on one key sending 64 chats (8 each) wrote **40** `PLAYER_CHAT` rows before this change (0.35 s) and **5** after (`WS_CHAT_BURST`; 0.11 s and 0.08 s on two runs). (d) `GET /players` returned every live player's position to any invited caller, and no client called it. The route is removed and dropped from the read-limit exemptions, so it now answers `404 {"error": "not found"}` like any unknown route. Against the `23b8830` `websocket.py`, 17 of the 23 tests in the new file fail. The 6 that pass are the open-world control, the three bucket-registry unit tests, the keyless-buckets test and the `/players` test. The 7 every-frame tests also fail against the batch's first `websocket.py`, and the writer test fails against the `23b8830` `rooms.py`. Against the pre-fix gate, the 4 latch tests in `test_guard.py` fail. Reading both clients' reconnect code (`static/explorer.js` `wsConnect`, `frontend/src/ws.js` `attachWorldSocket`) found no reconnect storm. After a 1008 close the first retry comes in about 1 s. Each refused (403) handshake never fires `onopen`, so the backoff climbs to its 30 s ceiling (±25% jitter). Neither client treats 1008 or 403 as final, and neither tells the player the invite stopped working. No client change in this batch. PR review (#137) found two more gaps, both fixed: while a fragmented message is open, `ws_recv` reads continuation and interleaved control frames without returning, so a revoked client that opened one fragment and then pinged stayed subscribed; `ws_recv` now takes an `on_frame` hook, and the loop passes the same throttled check, so each such frame is vetted and a refused one is not answered. And the fresh chat check ran before `moderation.screen()`, which can wait on a remote classifier, so a key revoked during screening still published; the chat path now re-checks fresh after screening, right before the broadcast and the row. +35 Python tests (31 in the new file: six from one parametrized frame-kind test, four pinning the re-check throttle, four covering revocation mid-message; 3 in `test_guard.py`; 1 in `test_server_rooms.py`). Against this branch's previous `websocket.py` and `protocol.py`, 3 of the 4 mid-message tests fail (the live-key control passes); with only the post-screen check removed, the screening test fails. Against the batch's per-frame `websocket.py`, 2 of the 4 throttle tests fail (the flood count and the in-interval ping); against a throttle without the fresh check, the 2 in-interval chat and move tests fail. Two existing tests were rewritten: one had relied on the gate reopening, and the other asserted `/players` returned 200. A second review of #137 (from the owner's account, request changes, on `a50ab58`) reproduced three more defects and two test gaps; all are fixed here. (e) Every check ran on a completed frame, and the 60 s idle timer reset on every recv, so a revoked client trickling one byte every 59 s never completed a frame and stayed subscribed to room broadcasts indefinitely (a 64 KiB frame at that rate lasts about 44 days). Once a frame's first byte arrives, the rest of it (header and payload) must now arrive within `protocol._FRAME_DEADLINE_SEC` (10 s), or the frame is a protocol error and the socket closes with 1002 through the normal disconnect path. Each recv inside a frame waits only for the time left, so nothing polls. The wait before a frame is still the idle timer, and the socket's timeout is restored after every frame, so fragmented messages are bounded per frame, not per message. Checking that bound found one more unchecked frame: the frame that opens a fragmented message is complete, but `ws_recv` read it without returning or calling `on_frame`, so it reset the idle timer once more unchecked. It is now vetted like the frames inside the message. The honest bound for a revoked socket is now: a move or chat its rate bucket admits closes it at once (a refused one counts as any other frame); any other complete frame closes it once 2 s have passed since its last check; a frame must finish arriving within 10 s of its first byte; and a socket that sends nothing is not checked and keeps receiving room broadcasts until the 60 s idle timeout. A hostile client can therefore stay subscribed for up to about 72 s after revocation (2 s + 60 s + 10 s); a browser client, which pings every 20 s, closes within about 20 s. G5's acceptance asks for a ban effective within 1 min, so that 72 s worst case is for #138 to weigh. The reviewer's optional stronger fix, a writer-side re-check before each broadcast, is not implemented. The `WS_REAUTH_INTERVAL_SEC` comment's claim that "a revoked listen-only session stays subscribed at most this long past its next frame" was untrue and is replaced, as are the matching statements in `fly-deployment.md` §6 (now evenly wrapped), the checklist's G5 evidence and the README invite-gate row. (f) If a key was revoked between the gate check and `guard.registered_name`, the upgrade fell into the keyless branch and joined under `?name=`, another player's registered name included, writing a `PLAYER_JOIN` row and broadcasting `player_join`. When no registered name comes back and the gate is active, the upgrade is now refused with the gate's own `403 {"error": "forbidden"}` before any row or broadcast. (g) The protocol-error close and the peer-close echo did not stop the writer first, so a queued data frame could follow those close frames (RFC 6455 §5.5.1); the 1008 path already prevented this. The `ProtocolError` handler now calls `stop_writer()` first, and `ws_recv` takes a `before_close` hook, which `websocket.py` sets to `player.stop_writer`. (h) Two mutations survived the suite: removing `stop_writer()` from `_close_unauthorized`, and removing the lock from `CredentialBuckets.allow` (the concurrency test never contended). A close-ordering test now records the writer's dead flag when `send_close` is called. The concurrency test swaps in a `TokenBucket` whose `allow()` sleeps between reading and writing its balance; without the lock it allowed **40**, **40** and **35** chats against a burst of 5 on three runs. This round adds +12 Python tests (9 in the new file, 3 in `test_server_protocol.py`), **47** for the batch. Against `a50ab58`'s `websocket.py` and `protocol.py`, with only the deadline constant added so the tests can patch it, 6 of the 11 frame-deadline, race and close-ordering tests fail; in the trickle test the revoked client received the other player's chat line. The 5 that pass are the quick-chunks, per-frame and idle-wait controls, the 1008 ordering test (that path was already right) and the restore-after-a-complete-frame unit test. Under named mutations, each new test fails: starting the deadline before the first byte fails the per-frame and idle-wait tests; leaving the idle timeout unrestored fails the idle-wait test and both socket-pair unit tests; dropping the partial-header read fails the quick-chunks test; dropping `stop_writer()` from `_close_unauthorized` or from the `ProtocolError` path, or the `before_close` hook, fails its own ordering test; removing the race refusal fails the race test (101 instead of 403); and leaving the opening fragment unchecked fails its test. **ADR-004 §7 amended (2026-11-03):** the latch changes §7's access rule, which said "With no key minted the gate is open". The gate now closes on the first key or the first registration invite and never reopens, and there is no supported way to reopen a world. That is an access change, a scoped decision under ADR-034 §5. The owner directed the merge on 2026-11-03 and delegated the open choices; ADR-004 carries a dated amendment note, and §7's two gate bullets now state the latch. The revocation bound (about 20 s for a browser client, about 72 s worst case for a hostile one) is accepted for beta; a follow-up adds a server-side re-check that does not wait for client frames.
  **Verified:** Python 3.11 (`.venv`). The session owner ran `./scripts/check.sh` on `e0a3344`: **2109 Python passed**, **138 Vitest passed**, production bundle fresh, installed wheel verified. The second PR review reported `./scripts/check.sh` exit 0 on `a50ab58` with **2113 Python** and **138 Vitest** passed, and CI green on `a50ab58`. After this round's fixes: Ruff passed on the whole tree; the 9 affected files (`test_ws_credential_lifetime`, `test_server_protocol`, `test_ws_conformance`, `test_server_rooms`, `test_guard`, `test_server`, `test_moderation`, `test_shared_text_fail_closed`, `test_day_one_recording`) gave **520 passed** (142.27 s); the full Python suite, `python -m pytest tests/ -q`, gave **2125 passed** (553.03 s, exit 0) in one run, with no WebSocket timeouts and no re-runs. `git diff --check` is clean. The session owner then ran `./scripts/check.sh` on `4b5e713` (this round's head, Python 3.11, Node 20.19): Ruff clean, **2125 Python passed** (533.2 s, one run), **138 Vitest passed**, production bundle byte-fresh, installed-wheel smoke passed (`enfolded-0.1.1rc2`). Playwright was not run locally; CI runs the browser smoke. No frontend or `static/` file changed. No live model calls.
  **Irreversibility check:** none — no migration, no golden re-pin, no new `world_mutations` write path (no `record_mutation` call site added or removed; a refused frame, a raced upgrade and a too-slow frame write nothing new), no world-meta pin, no era-bank edit. The only new SQL is a read-only `SELECT EXISTS`. The diff is the gate latch, the every-frame open-socket re-check and close, the per-frame arrival deadline, the upgrade-race refusal, the writer stop before every close frame, shared per-credential buckets, the `/players` removal, tests and operator docs. Removing `/players` can be undone: no client called it. The latch changes ADR-004 §7's access rule; it is not a one-way door, and the owner's scoped decision is recorded above and in ADR-004's 2026-11-03 amendment.

- **Off-host backups are stored in a private S3-compatible bucket, not in public GitHub Actions artifacts** (`.github/workflows/backup.yml`, `scripts/backup_gate.sh`, `scripts/backup_store.py`, `scripts/backup_health.py`, `tests/test_backup_health.py`, `tests/test_backup_store.py`, `docs/infrastructure/backup-bucket.md`, `docs/infrastructure/fly-deployment.md`, `docs/infrastructure/stack.md`, `docs/roadmap/README.md`, `docs/roadmap/phase-2-scale.md`, `docs/roadmap/next-steps-2026-q4.md`). The half-hourly job uploaded the live SQLite file, player chat included, with `actions/upload-artifact` and a 90-day retention. GitHub's list-artifacts endpoint says anyone with read access to the repository can use it, and this repository is public. Nothing is deployed, so that upload had not published a live database. The workflow no longer calls the artifact action, including when validation fails or the bucket is unset. Healthy objects are `offhost/worlds-backup-<app>-<UTC snapshot-start>.db`. A failed `validate_backup.py` still fails the run and stores `offhost/unvalidated-worlds-backup-<app>-<stamp>.db`. Quarantine keys, other apps, empty objects, and future stamps never satisfy freshness. Age is the stamp in the name, not `LastModified`. The client is AWS CLI **2.39.3**, pinned by the SHA-256 of the linux x86_64 zip `14cb35809ad7547dd3c69fa9899a3975fbd850b6a6791f3fe264856490cd9a63` and the aarch64 zip `f6eb845fbacb11bee87de3f5fbe8043773cbe89d0414607b44b71e216eb9226b`, installed only after the gate is ready. Addressing is path-style. Checksums are sent only when the operation requires them. Cloudflare R2 is an example of an S3-compatible store, and **30** days is the example used when `ENFOLDED_BACKUP_RETENTION_DAYS` is unset. Neither is a recorded decision, and this batch adds no ADR. ADR-005 still describes hour-stamped artifacts and 90-day artifact retention. The endpoint (it contains the account id) and the bucket are secrets, so they are masked in the public job log. Error text drops the endpoint, the bucket, and object keys, and the job summary does not print the key. `ENFOLDED_BACKUP_REQUIRED=true` with a missing Fly token or bucket setting fails the job. A typo in the required flag, region, retention, endpoint, or bucket exits 2, including an endpoint port of `:bad`, `:0`, `:65536`, or an empty port (`https://host:`). A delete response that is not a JSON object is a `StoreError`, not a traceback. Until those values are set, the schedule stays an inactive no-op and does not install the CLI or upload anywhere. Setting only `FLY_API_TOKEN` no longer uploads. The store step is `id: store`. Prune runs only when `steps.store.outcome == 'success'`, deletes with `delete-objects` in batches of **1000**, and never deletes the newest validated object for the app. Quarantine objects are not protected. `delete` of one named key can still remove that newest object. `delete-before` cannot: it keeps the newest nonempty validated object, refuses a cutoff in the future, takes `--after` so only the window that can hold redacted text is removed, and `--dry-run` lists without deleting. A zero-byte or non-integer `Size` is not the copy that floor protects. Listing uses the two per-app prefixes and continues past **100** pages, up to **250** (enough for 3,650 days at 48 snapshots a day). Any lifecycle rule deletes the last copy once the schedule has been stopped that many days; retention plus **7** only delays it by **7** days. `get` and `put` wait `ENFOLDED_BACKUP_S3_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (default **3600**; `0` or `none` waits until the process ends). List and delete stay at **120** seconds. The workflow job still ends at **20** minutes. A download is created mode **0600**, written to a `.partial` name, and removed on `BaseException`, including interrupt. `get` uses the write key even when the optional read pair is set. That pair is not list-only: an R2 Object Read token can download every backup, and health, prune, and `delete-before` list with it when both variables are set. A half-set pair fails closed. `backup_health.py` includes the redacted `StoreError` text. Redaction is case-insensitive and also covers the endpoint's first host label. The installer uses `curl -fsSL --retry 5 --retry-all-errors --connect-timeout 20 --max-time 300`. The unset retention of **30** is commented as an example in `backup_gate.sh` and `backup.yml`. `next-steps-2026-q4.md` adds the decision-table row for the destination, vendor, and retention (ADR-034 §5). The AWS child sets `AWS_REGION` to the configured region and drops every other inherited `AWS_*` value. `flyctl` is pinned to **0.5.1**. Checkout sets `persist-credentials: false`. The Fly token in the runbook is `fly tokens create deploy -a <app>`. The off-host restore path is `./worlds-backup.db` on both the upload and the restore. Assuming each snapshot is about **300 MB** (not measured; nothing is deployed), **1,440** copies are **432 GB-month**. At $0.015/GB-month that is **$6.48** before the included 10 GB-month and **$6.33** after it (**422** GB-month). The free tier covers about **6.9 MB** per copy (`10 / 1,440` GB), not a few hundred megabytes. At **500 MB** the same count is **720** GB-month, **$10.80** before the allowance and **$10.65** after it. Puts and lists stay inside the operation allowance, so that charge is **$0**. Review follow-up on #136: the review showed a 31-day-old sole backup pruned to zero objects. The retention prune now keeps that newest validated object. The first gate on this batch was **1918** Python tests (**47** backup cases). The first review follow-up added **11** cases (**58** in the two backup modules) and moved the suite from **1909** to **1929**. Reviews 5737068590 and 5732331506 are the follow-up in this entry's later sentences: future `delete-before`, the transfer timeout, the read-token docs, and the low findings. That follow-up adds **13** cases (**71** in the two backup modules). The suite is now **1942**. A local install of the pinned x86_64 CLI printed `aws-cli/2.39.3`. A `list-objects-v2` against `https://127.0.0.1:9` failed to connect and requested `https://127.0.0.1:9/enfolded-backups?list-type=2&prefix=offhost%2F`, which is path-style. That call used fixture credentials and uploaded nothing. No R2 bucket, GitHub Actions run of `backup.yml`, or restore from object storage was exercised.
  **Verified:** Python 3.11.18, Node 20.19.0. After reviews 5737068590 and 5732331506, `ENFOLDED_E2E=1 ./scripts/check.sh` exited 0 on the first run (2026-11-02T13:22:34Z–2026-11-02T13:31:46Z, 552s): Ruff passed; **1942 Python passed** (365.82s); **138 Vitest passed** (15 files, 847ms); production bundle byte-fresh (`index-vRwqOYno.js`); installed-wheel smoke verified `enfolded-0.1.1rc2` (`schema_version` **28**, `as_of_utc` 2026-11-02 13:28:46Z); Playwright **99 passed** (3.0m). Two `/world` writes logged `BrokenPipeError` when the browser client went away; those cases still passed. No live model calls and no live bucket upload. `git diff --check` clean. This entry was written after that run. The previous gate on this batch, after review 5732318666, was **1929 Python passed** (362.59s) and Playwright **99 passed** (3.0m). The first gate, before those review fixes, was **1918 Python passed** (364.24s) and Playwright **99 passed** (3.1m).
  **Irreversibility check:** none — no migration, no golden re-pin, no new `world_mutations` write path, no world-meta pin, no era-bank edit. The diff changes where an off-host copy is stored, how the gate, the prune, and the freshness check decide, and the operator docs. No ADR was added. - **Unknown places and non-string speech fields fail before any charge** (`server/handlers.py`, `server/websocket.py`). Follow-up to the #126 review, then the #128 reviews. `/speak` and `/agent/voice` resolve the place before `moderation.screen`: an unknown place is HTTP 404 `no such place in this world` and does not call the screen, the classifier, or either cap. A real place is still screened before the voice budget and any chronicle row. The AI kill switch still answers before that lookup. A present `message` that is not a string (`null`, `false`, `true`, `0`, `1`, `2.5`, `[]`, a non-empty list, `{}`) is the existing private 400 `A message is required.` and is not spoken. A missing message stays the authored prompt. The strings `"0"`, `"false"`, and `"null"` are unchanged. A line with nothing visible in it — whitespace, format characters such as U+200B, U+2060 or U+FEFF, soft hyphens, bidi marks, bare combining marks — is that same 400 (`_has_visible_text`, by Unicode category, not a list of code points). Nothing is removed from a line that is kept: an emoji joiner, a Persian ZWNJ or a Thai word break is screened, spoken and recorded as typed (`hel\u200blo` is judged as `hel\u200blo`), so the append-only chronicle never holds a rewritten line. `/puzzle/attempt` coerces `node_name` with `str(... or "")[:128]` and never 500s; `/puzzle` clips its name the same way. An empty or omitted name, and a value that coerces to `""` (`null`, `false`, `0`, `[]`, `{}`), still attempts the root. Any other name the world was not born with — `ghost-1`, `true`, `7`, `['x']` — is HTTP 404 on both GET `/puzzle` and POST `/puzzle/attempt` (one resolver, `_puzzle_target`), before `will_store_guess`, the screen, the attempt session, and any chronicle row; GET `/puzzle` used to answer any unknown name with the root's question. Existence is the store's answer, as on `/speak` and `/node`: a real place below the client's `depth` view is rebuilt at its own depth (one level deeper since #129), so both routes describe and judge the same question whatever depth the client loaded. The session key and the stored name are the real node. On seed 77 the root's attempt count stayed on one clock across those ghost names: a correct answer sent as `ghost-3` and `ghost-4` wrote **0** `PUZZLE_SOLVED` rows, and the real wrong guesses still failed at `max_attempts` with one `PUZZLE_FAILED`. WebSocket chat applies the same non-string rule: the sender gets a private `chat_declined` with `A message is required.`, and the text is not screened, broadcast, or stored. A frame whose JSON is not an object (`[]`, `"hi"`, `5`, `null`) is ignored like invalid JSON; it used to raise `AttributeError` into an HTTP 500 written onto the upgraded socket and one Sentry report per frame. Each GET and POST clears `_access_logged`, `_status`, `_resp_len`, and `_private_response` next to `_started`, so a kept-alive socket cannot reuse the previous response's log flag, status, length, or `Cache-Control: no-store`. `_on_timeout` reads that cleared `_status`. `handle_one_request` gives every later request on a kept-alive socket its own read budget: the wait for it is bounded by `NESTED_WORLDS_HTTP_READ_TIMEOUT`, and its clock starts when its first byte arrives, so it is never judged against time the previous request used (the budget was armed once per connection). The `anthropic` bucket and `NESTED_WORLDS_ANTHROPIC_*` names are unchanged. **+10** tests (1858 on `a461173` → 1868): six from the first round, four for the review fixes.

- **Shared text is held when moderation cannot judge it** (`server/moderation.py`, `consciousness/__init__.py` `classify_content`, `server/guard.py`, `server/handlers.py`, `server/websocket.py`, `server/participant_api.py`, `persistence/ideas.py`, `server/ideas_api.py`, `static/ideas.js`, `server/intervention_api.py`, `interface/__init__.py`, `main.py`, `server/__init__.py`, `evals/reporting.py`, ADR-004). Mark Weeks approved this failure rule on 2026-10-25. A shared line is published only after a local clean verdict or a finished reply whose stripped text is exactly one `ALLOW` or `BLOCK`, with an optional `Verdict:` label and light wrapping punctuation. `Not ALLOW`, `ALLOW but ...`, `BLOCKED`, and a second line are undecided. Empty, missing, and length-stopped replies are undecided. `max_tokens` for that call is **32**: the prompt asks for one token, and 32 leaves room for a short preamble to finish under a normal stop. A length stop stays undecided. The model id comes from `runtime.model_for("moderation")`. A timeout, a provider error, `NESTED_WORLDS_DISABLE_MODERATION`, a spent moderation budget, an undecided verdict, or a per-user cap holds text other players can see: no chronicle row, no broadcast, no visible idea, and no published profile. Registered names at `/register` and `invite mint` are refused the same way. An ungated WebSocket `?name=` is not screened. `play --name` is screened only when that session writes the canonical world. An unscreened local-mode name can still be `player_name` on chronicle rows in the same database. `invite mint --unscreened` is the operator override. Name checks do not charge a per-user cap, because a registration token is not authenticated yet. The author gets HTTP 200 and "The worlds hold those words for now. They are not shared. Say it another way, or try again later." A local or model BLOCK still uses the decline line. Neither charges the voice budget. A local clean line still publishes while the classifier is down. Empty text is allowed before the disabled-moderation hold, so a blank published profile still saves. Ideas use the same screen. A local block is HTTP **400**. A definitive hold is HTTP **422** with `held: true`: the composer stays editable, the pending receipt is cleared, and a reload does not submit again. An indeterminate Ideas failure stays HTTP **503**. A repeated request id is recognized before the screen, which adds one `identify` on submit; the ideas review count for a new submission is now **3**. Puzzle attempts still count and still record correctness. An unjudged guess is omitted from `PUZZLE_ATTEMPT` and from `answer_given` on `PUZZLE_FAILED`. The screen runs only after a name mismatch, a renewed question, and an already-solved puzzle have returned, and it skips a correct answer. An incorrect guess is screened as the same **64** characters a failure row can store. Stored rows are left as written. Private notes, unpublished profile text, movement, and scale actions continue. CLI `speak` is screened when the session seed is the canonical world, including the launch default **382** when the variable is unset. Empty, `off`, or `none` stays the operator terminal. Starting caps, to be measured: **600** moderation-triggering actions per credential (a local block does not spend it; a clean line still does, before that clean return; reset at UTC midnight), **60** classify calls per credential (checked before the shared **2000**), and **200** model-calling actions per credential. A spent shared moderation budget holds still-unjudged text for the whole cohort until the next UTC day. The persisted `anthropic` bucket and `NESTED_WORLDS_ANTHROPIC_*` names are unchanged. A missing credential stays open on these per-user gates. `moderation_call` logs `outcome` and leaves out the player text and any raw address. A classify failure logs that line once, including when the exception is raised outside `classify_content`. **56** behavior tests in this batch.

- **Plain HTTP reads, response writes, and handler threads are now bounded** (`server/__init__.py`, `server/handlers.py`, `server/guard.py`, `server/websocket.py`, `tests/test_server_limits.py`). The accept path had no socket read deadline, and `ThreadingMixIn` started a thread per connection with no cap. `request_queue_size` is only a listen backlog of 64. The WebSocket cap (default 128) applies after the upgrade request is read, so a stalled client never reached it. `NESTED_WORLDS_HTTP_READ_TIMEOUT` (default **30s**) bounds the request line, headers, and body from accept. A body that stalls after headers returns **408** `{"error":"request timed out"}`; a request line that never finishes is closed without a status. `NESTED_WORLDS_HTTP_WRITE_TIMEOUT` (default **30s**) is a separate stall timer: each accepted chunk re-arms it. CPython applies one socket timeout to an entire `sendall`, and a static file is one `wfile.write`, so using that timeout raw would cut a slow scene download at 30s even while the client kept reading. A client that keeps accepting bytes continues for as long as the transfer takes. A client that never reads fills the window and is dropped after one blocked send, which frees the handler slot. Thirty seconds is the same order as the read budget: long enough for congestion or a brief pause with a full window, short enough that a silent reader cannot pin a thread until restart. A sub-second default would drop a reader paused on a normal radio gap. `NESTED_WORLDS_MAX_HANDLER_THREADS` (default **160**, above the WebSocket default and the proxy hard limit of 150) refuses further connections with **503**, `Retry-After: 2`, and `{"error":"server busy"}` without reading the request. `/health` stays inside that cap. The refusal runs before any request is read, so exempting one path would mean reading on the accept thread, and a process that is refusing players should fail its health check. The write timeout is what keeps a silent reader from making that 503 last. The refusal log records `observability.hash_ip` of the client address only — no raw IP and no port. A finished upgrade still calls `sock.settimeout(60)`, which lifts both HTTP timers; application `ping`/`pong` and protocol ping/pong stay on that idle timer. Invalid and non-positive values keep the defaults. **16** behavior tests. **Verified:** After merging `origin/main` at `08bd47d` (#119, #120, #121, #123), `ENFOLDED_E2E=1 ./scripts/check.sh` on Python 3.11.17 and Node 20.19.0 exited 0 on the first post-merge run: Ruff passed; **1758 Python passed** (332.75s); **138 Vitest passed** (15 files, 1.24s); production bundle byte-fresh; installed-wheel smoke verified `enfolded-0.1.1rc2` (`schema_version` **28**); **98 Playwright passed** (3.1m) with system Chrome 156.0.8078.47 (`PW_CHROMIUM_PATH`). During that browser run, two `/world` writes logged `BrokenPipeError` through the existing dispatch boundary when the client went away; those cases still passed. Before the merge, focused `tests/test_server_limits.py` was **16 passed** (9.65s), including a cap of **1**, a **0.5s** write timeout, an unread `/media/places/orchard-v1.png` (2,611,199 bytes) with a clamped receive window, `/health` returning 503 then 200 inside **2.5s** without the stalled client closing first, and a slow read of `/media/score/pizzicato.mp3` (16,706 bytes) that completed past a **0.2s** write timeout. No failing post-merge gate.
  **Irreversibility check:** none — no migration, no golden re-pin, no new `world_mutations` write path, no world-meta pin, no era-bank edit. The diff is a request read deadline, a response-write stall timer, a hashed handler-cap refusal log, tests, and env documentation.

- **Idle players stay connected while they read or think** (`static/clientlogic.js`, `frontend/src/ws.js`, `static/explorer.js`, `server/websocket.py`). `sock.settimeout(60)` makes `recv` raise `TimeoutError` (an `OSError`), and the session loop treats that as disconnect. The loop already answers `{"type":"ping"}` with `{"type":"pong"}` and writes no chronicle row; neither browser client sent one. A server-sent RFC ping was left alone: a timeout can arrive mid-frame, and continuing the parser would desynchronize it, while a ping thread would add a third thread per connection beside the reader and writer. Both clients now share one **20s** application ping, one third of the 60s deadline. `arm()` replaces the interval so a reconnect cannot stack timers; `stop()` runs on close and on the React effect cleanup. The timer is not paused when the tab is hidden — the socket clients do not use page visibility, only the scene renderer skips hidden frames, and pausing here would drop the session at 60s. A browser that later clamps timers to one minute can still miss that deadline; the 60s bound was not raised. The shipped bundle grew **343,783 → 344,456** bytes (`index-CPW0XqpC.js` → `index-vRwqOYno.js`); the CSS hash is unchanged. Message shapes are unchanged.
  **Verified:** After merging `origin/main` (#119 and #120), `ENFOLDED_E2E=1 ./scripts/check.sh` on Python 3.11.17 and Node 20.19.0 exited 0 on the first run: Ruff passed; **1,731 Python passed** (305.57s); **138 Vitest passed** (15 files, 904ms); production bundle byte-fresh (`index-vRwqOYno.js`); installed-wheel smoke verified `enfolded-0.1.1rc2`; **98 Playwright passed** (3.0m). `frontend/` and `static/` were unchanged on main, so the bundle was not rebuilt ahead of the gate; the gate's own build matched the committed bytes. No rerun.

Hosted restore, live-model quality, target devices, and participant research are not certified by these checks.
  **PR #109 review:** addressed 11 findings. Raw backup configuration now rejects typos; 30-minute copies retain the 60-minute freshness limit. Validation failures retain a quarantined artifact while failing the run. Freshness requires a completed successful run of the trusted workflow on main with matching repository/commit metadata; the redundant within-run check is removed because a run cannot certify its own final success. Explicit read-only SQLite URIs and closing prevent platform assumptions and leaked handles. The DNS fix now lives on the shared server, shared accounts/HTTP fixtures live in conftest, the Python process helper reports stderr and cleans up startup failures, and pilot envelopes reject extra fields. A handshake-only backlog control sometimes passed before asynchronous resets arrived; the corrected regression queues **32 requests** and proves they stay connected, passing at 64 and failing at 5. Focused tooling checks passed **55 tests**, server/recovery/participant/Ideas/intervention checks passed **83**, and final startup checks passed **6**. **Verified after review:** canonical gate exited 0 — Ruff passed, **1,417 Python passed** (246.97s), **131 Vitest passed**, production bundle byte-fresh, installed-wheel smoke passed, and **96 Playwright passed** (2.7m). Actionlint 1.7.12, shell syntax, protocol JSON, 83 local document targets and diff whitespace passed. The revised live artifact checker returned missing; hosted recovery remains unproven.

- **Exploration has one readable navigation surface and an interface that changes with the place** (`static/interface.{js,css}`, `static/navigation.js`, `static/map.css`, scene/map/Act views, asset packaging and browser regressions; [evaluation](evaluation/2026-09-21-ux-visual-language.md)). Five before/after comparisons cover four scales and two different Objects. Visual review corrected awkward 200% word breaks that passed overflow assertions, overly strong engraved grain, inherited pollen masking current fog, and an off-screen selected map marker after resize. Browser tests also exposed passage buttons being replaced between pointer-down and click by unrelated presence updates; stable contents now retain controls and focus. Request failures remain recoverable in place, keyboard combination toggles retain focus, and scene/map reading surfaces remain independent of bright or missing artwork.   **PR #107 review:** addressed all 17 findings on `5a36650`. Restored Wayback label contrast, observation-meter geometry, passage conditions and distinct player/inhabitant cues; retained map puzzle drafts and recorded progress; preserved authored HTTP refusals and safe retries. Both exploration columns now scroll normally, history travel hands focus to the destination, resize preserves in-view pan/zoom, and the selected marker says “You are here” without duplicating identity. Removed dead props/styles and consolidated UX/history server launchers with newline framing and cleanup. The initial map probe reported 4,210 palette calls for 4,208 nodes and **32.5 ms**, but omitted one hidden call; neither number covered all resolver work. The correction is recorded below. Thirteen browser scenarios failed on the reviewed head; the suite adds those and one resolver-work bound. The loading-focus fix initially blocked choices after a failed read; the retry path now replaces that focused loading surface. Visual evidence and all review dispositions are in the evaluation.
  **Re-review follow-up:** the re-review of `65b9429` raised two findings, both fixed here. `apply` resolved through the module-local binding, so a probe on the exported reference could not see it and the selected place resolved three times: the asserted **4,210** calls were the observable subset of a real **4,211**. `selectNode` now resolves once and hands those tokens to both the page application and the marker, `apply` resolves through the exported reference when a caller supplies none, and the total is asserted as equality — **4,210** calls for **4,208** nodes, every one visible. Separately, a same-node `error` → `loading` change removed the focused retry button and dropped keyboard focus to `<body>`; the keyed lookup now falls back to the destination heading, as the Act composer already did. Two browser cases pin both and fail on the unfixed source, the observability case reporting **0** observed resolutions. The surprise worth recording: the count case alone could never have caught this, because the hidden resolution kept the printed total looking correct — only a case that probes the resolver reference exposes it.
  **Remaining cleanup (September 23):** all 20 PR threads were already resolved, including the nonblocking Chronicle report; no formal approval was recorded. The alleged development StrictMode crash was not reproduced, but an actual React development fixture reproduced lost opener focus on both Close and Escape. Cleanup now closes the retained native dialog before restoring the opener, and setup opens only a closed dialog. Both regressions fail on `5283559` and pass with the correction, including reopening. The rebuilt scene was also inspected directly in a disposable world: both close paths restore focus, and Enter reopens Chronicle. PR/evaluation status, the visual-language guide and the roadmap now reflect these dispositions and the next review handoff.
  **Pre-merge accessibility pass (September 23):** a further review of `d324275` raised three findings, all confirmed and fixed before merge. Two were **regressions from this batch's own presence fix**: converting the traveler rows to keyboard-reachable buttons left them focusable while `player_join` and `agent_enter` still hold an empty destination, and the delegated handler ignores an empty `data-node`, so a keyboard player could reach a control that did nothing while the React client already guarded it. Both map rows are disabled until a destination arrives. Third, combination choices carried `aria-pressed` although `choose` appends a step rather than toggling one, so a control reported as pressed appended a duplicate and stayed pressed; the attribute is removed and the accessible name deliberately kept, because the composer matches retained focus by that string. Two browser cases fail on `d324275`, reporting **enabled** and **"false"** respectively. The lesson worth carrying: making a control keyboard-reachable is not free — it turns every inactive state into a reachable dead end unless it is also disabled.

  **Review corrections (PR #106 review, two blocking and four cheap findings):** the participant recap in `persistence/participants.py` unioned every `intervention_work` row into its place set, so a queued v3 receiver was disclosed with its material history before any consequence arrived there, contradicting ADR-028's "pending work is labeled without destination names"; the union now takes completed receivers only, pinned by the dual-client settlement test (the Universe enters Ada's recap only after hop 1 lands). v3 settlement voiced outcomes as property literals (`Gather: Observed: asteroid belt is True.`) in the chronicle, history narration and Act status, the same defect class as the #105 narration corrections; `multiverse/interventions_v3.py` now carries a **33**-line `_OBSERVED` bank for the declarative operators, keeps `apply_verb`'s authored line (with the node's aspect clause) for the **11** verb operators, stores the line as `note` on each outcome, and `describe()` never formats a property. `server/intervention_api.py` fingerprinted null `delegate`/`performer`/`actor_identity` keys that `authorize()` ignores, so a lost-ack retry without the null key got 409 for an attempt that had already changed the world; null keys now stay out of the fingerprint while a named performer still mismatches. A v1/v2 commit with no `steps` key had regressed from 400 to 409; the unconditional list check is restored on that branch. The preview-route delegation assertions in `tests/test_expressive_interventions.py` passed vacuously once the route closed and now pin the refusal text instead. A new test pins `_ATTEMPTS` and `_OBSERVED` coverage against the v2 operator set so a future `EXTRA` verb cannot turn a scale's Act into a `KeyError`. All six regressions were run against the unfixed head first and failed for the stated reasons. Two Copilot findings on the same PR: the accept response derived `phase` and its pending line from the origin's maturation delay alone, so an instant settlement (every level below Planetary System) that still queued outward hops answered `observed` while `recent()` reported `pending` for the same attempt; both now follow the pending count, pinned in the direct-attempt test. The Act help named three clarification classes and now names action, target, scope and order, matching the guide and ADR-028; the bundle is rebuilt (`index-RVeBs2aa` → `index-DQM8sSda`). The first push of these corrections (`bb729c8`) turned CI's Browser E2E smoke red, **2 of 68** cases: the delivery test had pinned the mechanical outcome line `star density is 360` that the voice fix replaced. Reproduced locally with the same two failures; the test now expects the authored Scatter line and the settled value stays asserted through `/node`. The two review decisions were then taken in the ADR's favour. Receiver propagation: `interventions_v3.receive()` zeroed the outgoing strength whenever the receiver's own delta was empty, so a repeated identical wave into a saturated echo with no integer `danger_level` (every scale above Region) stopped the chain where v2 passed **0.65** onward and ADR-028 says the observed remainder feeds the next hop; the remainder now travels on regardless of the receiver's stored delta, the arrival line says so, ADR-028 names the saturated case explicitly, and a delivery test drives a Scatter through a pre-saturated Universe to a pending hop at the Multiverse root. Receipts: a quiet reply given while the interpreter was unavailable stays that request ID's final receipt, as ADR-028's trade-off already states, because a provisional receipt would let a concurrent same-ID duplicate accept after the client had minted a new ID; the contract is now written for API clients in `docs/development/discovery-return.md` and `persistence/intents.py`, and the race test additionally pins that a same-ID retry makes no model call while a new ID reaches the model.
  **Verified:** Ruff; `tests/test_commit_discover.py` **16** and `tests/test_expressive_interventions.py` **26** passed; the six regressions failed on the unfixed head first. `ENFOLDED_E2E=1 ./scripts/check.sh` after the Copilot and e2e corrections: Ruff, **1,336 Python** (315.84 s), **122 Vitest** (13 files), byte-fresh production bundle, installed-wheel smoke and **68 Playwright** (2.9 min) passed; the delivery spec reproduced CI's two failures locally before the expectation change. After the two decisions: Ruff, **1,337 Python** (321.75 s), **122 Vitest**, byte-fresh bundle, installed-wheel smoke and **68 Playwright** (3.0m) passed; the two receiver regressions failed on the unfixed head first. No migration or ADR text changed. **Irreversibility check:** none — no migration, no golden re-pin, no new `world_mutations` write path; a read-side recap filter, an authored voice bank on the existing v3 settlement writer, two request-validation corrections and tests.
  **Final review follow-up:** outcome clauses now follow actual changed fields: Cultivate at minimum danger no longer claims a danger reduction, Relax at maximum wavelength no longer claims a redward shift, and five compound legacy verbs receive equivalent v3-only narration fixes with their aspect clauses preserved. Six remaining threads are addressed: predicted-availability fields and the dead UI branch removed; legacy `accept()` rejects v3 explicitly; receipt lookup shares one fingerprint check; recap data is decoded once; blank intentions cause no write requests; and `interventions.signal` retains the latest observed v3 signal so continuation is independent of chronicle content. Pre-upgrade v3 work has a retained-history fallback; absent legacy input stays pending for recovery. No migration is needed. - **Expand scale-native agency and connect place, sound and evolving description** (`multiverse/{interventions_v2,senses,description}.py`, `persistence/interventions.py`, migration 0027, `server/intervention_api.py`, both clients and local media; [ADR-027](decisions/ADR-027-expressive-world.md), [ADR-028](decisions/ADR-028-scale-native-autonomy.md), [initial evaluation](evaluation/2026-09-20-expressive-world.md), [correction evidence](evaluation/2026-09-20-scale-native-correction.md)). The existing Act surface now offers four actions at each of eleven scales, 44 real property transitions, optional ordered combinations and an intention entry point. Acceptance and consequences retain receipts, versioned semantics and transactional completion fences; new forced delegation and scripted gallery choices are refused while historical work remains truthful. Four curated generated images, eleven CC0 recordings and eleven musical forms connect presentation to node conditions. Eleven full-graph 16-second renders measured **0.1651–0.4698 peak / 0.0223–0.0879 RMS**, finite and non-clipping; this does not establish listener recognition or artistic acceptance. Scale, name, position and evolving description now share one identity block; duplicate scene headings, presence text, Speak headings and raw-aspect displays are removed. Descriptions derive from current properties and retained material traces without rewriting the born aspect; other players' actions refresh them and historical snapshots reconstruct them.
  The owner rejected the initial universal composer, forced performer selection, scripted gallery pane and insufficiently distinct music despite passing tests; those findings drove the replacement above. Verification also caught a redundant node-refresh loop, non-idempotent restart fixtures and a legacy address-location assertion. The design records now require commit-and-discover and interface styling derived from each node's evolving conditions, with color carrying learnable meaning. **Those two runtime corrections remain pending:** previews still expose material deltas/future routes, and interface styling remains largely fixed. Live-model intention quality, alignment/contribution scoring, agent vocabulary parity and the proposed beta artwork pipeline are not established. [The session handoff](roadmap/expressive-world-next-steps.md) separates dedicated UI/navigation work from behavioral and production gaps; this review checkpoint is not completion of the production-quality milestone.
  **Review corrections (PR #105 blocking findings):** the new `INTERVENTION_COMMITTED` / `INTERVENTION_ARRIVED` rows had no narration, so every chronicle, history-feed and node-voice line for the primary action path fell to the mechanical "something happened at <place>" default; `multiverse/history.py` now narrates both kinds from their authored flavor (commit phases `action`/`accepted`, hop phases `outcome`/`arrival`), `persistence/history.py` links an arrival to its commit as the cited source, and `static/clientlogic.js` carries the same fallback for both clients. `static/score.js` called `AudioParam.cancelAndHoldAtTime` without feature detection; Firefox lacks it, so a scale change with a sounding voice threw inside a React effect and unmounted `/app`. It now falls back to `cancelScheduledValues` plus `setValueAtTime`. The composer re-armed outcome polling only on success, so one failed poll (a 429 from the 120/min shared read limiter) ended it for the rest of the visit; `static/interventions.js` now re-arms from the last known pending count, at 8 s after a failure instead of 4 s. `intervention_work` was invisible to `work-report`, `inspect_work` and `retry_work`; `persistence/recovery.py` reports it from schema 27 with hop order as predecessor waits, `persistence.__init__` admits it to the operator helpers, and the recovery runbook lists it. `advance()` retried a failed hop every 30 s forever with a full traceback each time; it now uses the same capped exponential backoff as `deliver_work` (1, 2, 4 … 300 s), still with no attempt limit so no promise is discarded, and logs the traceback once. The delivery browser test's chronicle assertion, dropped in this batch, is restored against the settle line.
  **Covenant corrections (same review):** the intention path broke "failure stays in fiction": kill switch, missing key, exhausted Anthropic budget and provider failure all raised into the 409 handler. `server/intervention_api.py` now answers those with HTTP 200, `ai: false`, the authored line (`guard.QUIET_RESPONSE` for budget) and `steps: []`, the same shape `/speak` uses; a model that answers and declines returns `ai: true, declined: true` with its line; player-shaped mistakes (forced performer, unknown operator) stay 409. The composer shows a step-less reply as its status line. `static/sensory.js` drove particle drift, plate sway and echo rings from `performance.now()`, against the determinism contract's screenshot clause; it now runs on a frame counter (nominal 60/s), transients progress from the frame they first appear, and `tests/test_frontend_contract.py` polices `sensory.js` and `score.js` for `Math.random`, `Date.now` and `performance.now` (the score still schedules on the AudioContext clock). The node voice's cross-modal mirrors still described the replaced `nodeart.js` form families and `nodesound.js` modes; `consciousness/__init__.py` now derives appearance from the served senses block (plate or stratified/helical bands of the material, atmosphere, woven rings, remembered motif, scars) and sound from a Python mirror of `score.js` `SCORE_PROFILES` (form title, instrument, minor turn from polarity/tension, woven figure), for both the place's own voice and the inhabitant context. A contract test parses `SCORE_PROFILES` from the JS and pins the mirror equal to it.
  **Budget corrections (same review):** every material revision re-fetched a paid image: `SceneView.jsx` keyed its `/image` effect on `senses.revision`, and `imageprompt.style_signature` hashed up to 32 properties including per-arrival resonance state, so one three-hop action could charge four new fal.ai generations per viewer against the 60/day per-user and 200/day global caps. The effect now keys on the place and whether it has a curated plate, and the cache signature follows the first six scalar properties plus baseline, mood and aspect while the prompt still reads all 32; the shared renderer reinterprets live senses on top of the stable plate. Each single-action click posted `/interventions/preview` from the shared 20/min write budget; the choices read now ships each available action's full preview (steps, expected digest, changes, route), the composer uses it without a request, and only sequences and free-text intentions post. `App.jsx` refreshed `msg.node` for every connected client on each intervention notice, four `/node` reads per action per client; it now guards on the current node like scale acts. The `/world` tree carried a full senses block per node (about 194 KB of prose on a 72 KB depth-6 payload): both clients render the selected node from that tree, so the block stays, and JSON replies of 1 KB or more are now gzip-compressed for clients that accept it (depth-4 tree: more than 4× smaller in the regression test) with `Vary: Accept-Encoding`; small replies and non-accepting clients are unchanged. Content-versioned media and hashed bundle assets now carry `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`; pages and unversioned scripts do not. The composer reloaded on every fresh node object because its host spread a copy per call; it now reloads only on a changed served revision or identity. The delivery e2e read bound tightens from below 9 to below 8 `/node` reads per co-viewer, measured at 7 and 6.
  Compression exposed a latent race in `/app`: when the current place has no loaded passages, a background world reload one level deeper replaces the navigation stack with that tree, and a reload requested before another player's action could land after the notice-driven `/node` refresh and put the older description back ("engraved" reverting to "polished"; the expressive identity-block browser case failed 2 of 8 single runs and once in the full spec on the first budget head). `App.jsx` now orders node refreshes against world reloads: any node refreshed after a reload was requested is overlaid on that reload's tree. The case then passed 8 of 8 single runs.
  **Cleanup (same review):** `multiverse/interventions.py`, a facade whose docstring named v1 as the "current proposal vocabulary" while no runtime caller imported it, is deleted; `persistence.interventions.INTERPRETERS` remains the one map of accepted versions, and the v1-promise test now patches v2 to prove accepted v1 work keeps its own interpreter. `static/nodesound.js`, replaced by `score.js` in both clients but still served, wheel-verified and unit-tested, is deleted with its route, manifest entry and tests; the serving test now covers `score.js` and asserts the old path is 404, the wayback test pins the score revision to the served senses instead, and README, the beta brief and both client comments name the score and scene modules. `EnfoldedClient.entryHash` is exported and `score.js` hashes through it; the composer's node labels use `EnfoldedClient.displayName`; both local copies (same regex, same FNV-1a constants) are gone.
  **Copilot findings closed by the same review pass:** request-shape mistakes on the intervention routes (non-string place, non-list steps, non-string intention, non-integer version) were 409 like world conflicts; they are now the client's 400 (`server/intervention_api.py::Malformed`, plus `TypeError`), while valid shapes that conflict with the world (unknown action, stale preview, wrong position, closed vocabulary) stay 409. The composer's commit callback discarded the acceptance result, so its host re-read the node with no event key while the broadcast re-read it keyed on `event_id`; the composer now hands the result to `changed`, both hosts forward it as the refresher notice, and the two become one coalesced read. The scene glow's `globalAlpha` could exceed 1 at full energy, which canvas ignores, leaving the previous alpha in force; it is clamped. The provider-image CSP finding was already met on this head (`img-src` allows `fal.media` on the `/app` page; the explorer never requests generated images).
  **Independent review follow-up:** verified all 24 resolved PR threads against `0cbe690` and confirmed its hosted Python, frontend and browser jobs passed. The focused local review passed 159 Python and 118 Vitest cases, then found two follow-on regressions: the standalone listening script opens the script-free guide, so moving the score hash into `EnfoldedClient` caused an `entryHash` exception; it now explicitly loads the shared helper before rendering, without joining the world. All eleven 16-second excerpts render again at the same measured peak/RMS ranges above. Compression used a substring match, sending gzip even for `gzip;q=0` or `notgzip`, and missing case-insensitive/wildcard acceptance. It now parses exact tokens and weights, with explicit refusal overriding the wildcard. Six of seven new HTTP cases fail on the previous code; all nine transport cases pass after the fix. This follows [RFC 9110 section 12.5.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#name-accept-encoding). Final canonical verification: Ruff; **1,317 Python tests**; **118 Vitest tests**; byte-fresh bundle; installed-wheel smoke; **62 Playwright cases** (2.5 minutes), all passed. These follow-ups change transport and a read-only listening tool only; no persistence, history or world-rule change.
  **Second Copilot pass (after the PR left draft):** a missing hashed `/app/assets/*` file was answered with the SPA shell and, after the immutable-cache change above, cached for a year under a script URL; missing hashed assets are now 404 while application routes still get the shell. `advance()` fetched its 32 candidates in id order before checking predecessors, so a run of arrangements whose first hop keeps failing could fill every batch with their due-but-blocked successors and starve later independent work; candidates now sort unblocked-first (successors still ride in the same batch when there is room, so one arrangement still lands in one pass), pinned by a 17-arrangement regression. The recap listed the eight newest records, so an older unsettled commitment could hide behind eight settled ones and the composer would stop polling for it; unsettled commitments now lead the recap. A failed first `/interventions` read left the Act surface showing only the error for the rest of the visit; it now offers "Listen again", which reloads. Unavailable actions exposed their reason only through a tooltip on a disabled control; the reason is now visible helper text and part of the accessible name.

- **Ambition audit — where Enfolded is boxed in, and what a model-forward Enfolded looks like** ([assessment](evaluation/2026-10-03-ambition-and-boundaries.md); 2026-10-03). Owner-directed strategic review of the code, all 28 ADRs, roadmap/design/pitch documents, and a same-day survey of neural game engines, generative media, agent-society research, shipped AI-native games, and current Claude pricing. Finds the permanence architecture is not the constraint (the sacred layer is the append-only chronicle, with plain traces through `record_mutation` and every substance change through one atomic delta API, immutable born rows, write-time deltas, one canonical seed, one pinned hinge) and that the world is rigid by policy and by three early choices the sacred layer does not depend on: identity equal to the display name (`world_nodes.path` exists but no history reader joins through it), eleven scales enumerated in ~20 modules, and a model confined to four call sites (`speak` at `max_tokens=256`, agent voice at 200, moderation, an enum-bounded intent schema) with a 44-line FSM and zero `anthropic` references under `agents/`. Measured context: production never deployed and the chronicle empty; 0 external players and 3 GitHub issues, all owner-authored; default voice model `claude-opus-4-8` at $5/$25 per MTok against Opus 5.5 at $4/$20 with $0.20 cache reads; PixiJS declared but constructing nothing (the scene is 109 lines of 2D canvas). External survey: Genie has no API and a 60-second cap, Decart's world-model API costs ≈$72 per viewer-hour, World Labs Marble generates persistent splat worlds at ≈$1.20 each with MIT browser renderers, and every 2026 multiplayer world-model result converged on a canonical state server with per-client rendering. A list-price cost model puts an always-on, fully model-driven Enfolded at ≈$700–3,500/month by model tier as the ceiling; the recommended attention-gated design has a fixed floor of ≈$60–210/month plus ≈$0.18–0.86 per player-hour, and a one-hour session costs $0.10–0.58 in voice alone. Revised after the owner's cost question and Copilot's review: the always-on figure had been presented without the gated floor, the atomic-API wording was narrowed to substance deltas, and a stale 908 test count was replaced with the 1,358 Python tests collected on this branch. The owner's review then corrected three further claims: paid media regenerates per render key (per structural event for splats, per material-class change for plates), not once per place, so D3 carries a recurring line and D4 is only the sole source of ongoing *cognition* spend; model-authored puzzles need a new runtime validator with procedural fallback, because `puzzles/quality.py` is an ecology census and leak screening lives inside the procedural generator; and redaction scrubs five text fields without repairing applied deltas, so D2 requires compensating events rather than redaction. At the owner's request the batch also drafts [ADR-029](decisions/ADR-029-evolution-grammar.md) (Proposed, not ratified): identity moves to the existing `(world_seed, path)` key with names as time-bounded aliases, seven structural event kinds (`NODE_BORN`/`FRONTIER_GROWN`, `NODE_RENAMED`, `NODE_REASPECTED`, `PASSAGE_OPENED`/`CLOSED`, `NODE_RETIRED`/`REOPENED`, `LAW_SHIFTED`, `ERA_TURNED`) each written through the ADR-009 atomic path with an authority and an inverse, a deterministic validator (tree containment, breadth ≤ 9, depth ≤ 11, hinge-lineage immunity, seal gating at edge transit, pending-work blocks on retirement), operator-only triggering, and three implementing batches of which the identity layer must precede first production history. It also drafts [ADR-030](decisions/ADR-030-render-contract.md) (Proposed, not ratified): one sensory description feeding a renderer registry (`canvas`/`score`/`nodeart` free at `revision`, `plate` and `cue` at a `material` key that drops history-derived modifiers from today's `style_signature`, `volume` at a `structural` key, an `ephemeral` class reserved and unshipped), canonical-by-cache with recorded provenance so the determinism contract narrows to the procedural renderers, a first-party `render_assets` store replacing expiring provider URLs, bounded background generation with dollar budgets per renderer, the existing quiet-line failure path, the ADR-028 differentiation gate extended to paid renderers, and Wayback admitting an asset only on an exact key match with a second honesty clause. And it drafts [ADR-031](decisions/ADR-031-model-authorship.md) (Proposed, not ratified): the model proposes under frozen JSON schemas into an append-only proposal ledger, deterministic code validates (schema for shape and enums, code for numeric bounds and covenants), a per-tier policy ratifies (automatic with sampled human audit for interpretation; an operator queue graduating by measured rejection rate for bounded canon; operator-only for structure and physics, with retirement and law shift never automatic), and commits land only through the existing doors (ADR-009 deltas, ADR-029 events, `puzzle_instances` versions, ADR-030 direction versions) carrying ADR-029's `ratified` authority; corrections are compensating events, never redaction or deletion; a second model may reject prose but never ratify; disclosure is per surface, never per trace. Finally it drafts [ADR-032](decisions/ADR-032-inhabitant-cognition.md) (Proposed, not ratified): three cognition layers with the existing FSM authoritative (reflex every tick; one structured intention per tick only while a human is on stage, choosing only among candidates the FSM offered; Batch reflection off stage into an append-only `inhabitant_memory` of cited claims with enumerated hedges, opaque participant keys and no journal content), agency parity through the same acceptance APIs with an `inhabitant` participant identity and no delegation in either direction, real puzzle attempts replacing the dice roll while the agent-progress covenant stands unchanged, one bounded unsolicited line per visit, an open roster where external agents join through the same player surface over MCP with no privileged call, governance in code (single bottleneck, repetition demotion, collusion test, per-layer kill switches), and a dollar ceiling with the first batch shipping dark behind a flag for the pilot cohort. And it drafts [ADR-033](decisions/ADR-033-scale-registry.md) (Proposed, not ratified): one versioned charter per scale replacing the ~25 literal tables across ~20 modules, with charter set version 1 a byte-for-byte transcription so births, voices, puzzles, art and sound are proven identical at both golden depths and no re-pin occurs; the eleven-scale ladder fixed (ADR-008 stands) with lateral *kinds* as charter overlays that cannot change identity, breadth, mechanics or ordinal; `kind = common` for every born place and kind changes only through ADR-029's re-aspect along registry-permitted transitions; a validator that makes "scale is meaning" mechanical (disjoint form words, name-space bound, subtype schemas, every verb changing a real property, distinct forms per scale, a cap on kinds); the canonical world's charter set version pinned write-once in `world_meta`; and charter text proposable under ADR-031's tiers with new kinds operator-ratified and new scales never proposable. Recommends against a neural engine as substrate for 12 months and for neural rendering of chronicled state plus model authorship under ratification; names the identity/alias and provenance schema as the only change that must precede first production history; closes with a nine-item decision agenda and a 90-day sequence. Findings and proposals only; no covenant, ADR status, or runtime behavior changes. Verified: all 149 cited `path:line` references and relative links across the assessment and the five ADRs (76/14/19/6/9/25) resolved against the working tree by script on every commit, the only unresolved paths being the three new files the ADRs propose (`tests/test_evolution.py`, `tests/test_render_assets.py`, `multiverse/registry.py`); `pytest --collect-only` reports 1,358 tests on the branch; CI (Python tests, frontend build, browser smoke) green on every head through `427f0a9`; no code or tests changed; `./scripts/check.sh` not run for this documentation-only batch. Copilot's second review (14 findings) corrected cross-ADR consistency in the same batch: multi-row structural events share an `event_id`, render rejection is an append-only decision row, generated media sits beneath the procedural canvas, memory claims belong to `inhabitant_memory` and outward text to `authored_text`, committed proposals are retained as long as the canon referencing them, `participants.kind` is an additive discriminator defaulting to `human`, rumours and first words land only in Speak, and `kind = common` applies only to places born before kinds. The owner's follow-up review added two more: corrections commit only through the state-dependent path with transactional preconditions on every field, alias and structural fact they touch, and a conflict is refused into an explicit compensating decision against current state rather than a blind inverse; and the volume key includes the scale's scene-defining anchors with a mismatched volume suppressed until re-rendered, so an ordinary fracture or fold can never leave an obsolete volume on screen. **Irreversibility check:** none — no migration, no golden re-pin, no new `world_mutations` write path, no `world_meta` pin, no era-bank edit; the seven-file diff adds one evaluation document, five Proposed ADRs (029–033) and this entry, and each ADR lists the doors its implementing batches would trip.
